She had no more doubt of his coming to the dinner than she had of the reason why he came. And was it possible that she was being drawn into something very near a flirtation with a man twenty years her senior; a politician from Illinois; a huge, ponderous, grey-eyed, bald senator, with a Websterian head, who lived in Peonia? The idea was almost too absurd to be credited; but on the whole the thing itself was rather amusing. “I suppose senators can look out for themselves like other men,” was her final conclusion. She thought only of his danger, and she felt a sort of compassion for him as she reflected on the possible consequences of a great, absorbing love at his time of life. Her conscience was a little uneasy; but of herself she never thought. Yet it is a historical fact that elderly senators have had a curious fascination for young and handsome women. Had they looked out for themselves too? And which parties most needed to be looked after?
===Chinese===
他走后，她在房间里踱来踱去，思前想后。她看透了拉特克利夫突然变调的含义。他会来赴宴，这一点她毫不怀疑，正如对他为何而来毫不怀疑一样。而她自己，难道真有可能正被卷进一场近乎调情的勾当，对象是个比她大二十岁的男人；一个伊利诺伊来的政客；一个身材高大、体格魁伟、灰眼睛、秃头顶、长着一颗韦伯斯特式脑袋、住在皮奥尼亚的参议员？这念头荒唐得几乎叫人难以相信；不过大体说来，事情本身倒颇有意味。“想来参议员也同别的男人一样，自会照管自己，”这是她最后的结论。她只想到他的危险；一想到在他这把年纪，一场倾心夺魄的伟大爱情可能招致的后果，她便对他生出几分怜悯。她的良心微微不安；至于她自己，是从不想的。然而年老的参议员对年轻貌美的女子自有一股说不清的魅惑，这可是有史可稽的事实。他们可也照管好了自己？而两造之中，究竟哪一造更该被人照管呢？

===Original===
When Madeleine and her sister arrived at Welckley’s the next Saturday evening, they found poor Schneidekoupon in a temper very unbecoming a host.

“He won’t come! I told you he wouldn’t come!” said he to Madeleine, as he handed her into the house. “If I ever turn communist, it will be for the fun of murdering a senator.”
===Chinese===
下一个礼拜六晚上，马德琳姐妹抵达韦尔克利餐馆时，发现可怜的施奈德库彭正大发一通脾气，全无主人应有的风度。

“他不来了！我说过他不会来的吧！”他一边搀马德琳进门，一边对她说。“我哪天要是当了共产主义者，准是为了图个痛快，好谋杀个把参议员。”

===Original===
Madeleine consoled him gently, but he continued to use, behind Mr. Clinton’s back, language the most offensive and improper towards the Senate, and at last, ringing the bell, he sharply ordered the head waiter to serve dinner. At that very moment the door opened, and Senator Ratcliffe’s stately figure appeared on the threshold. His eye instantly caught Madeleine’s, and she almost laughed aloud, for she saw that the Senator was dressed with very unsenatorial neatness; that he had actually a flower in his buttonhole and no gloves!
===Chinese===
马德琳温言抚慰他，他却兀自躲在克林顿先生背后，对参议院使用最无礼、最不成体统的言辞；最后他按下铃，厉声吩咐领班上菜。恰在那一刻，门开了，拉特克利夫参议员堂堂的身影出现在门槛上。他的目光霎时与马德琳相接，她险些笑出声来，因为她看见参议员收拾得整洁过人，全无半点参议员气派；扣眼里居然真插着一朵花，而且没有戴手套！

===Original===
After the enthusiastic description which Schneidekoupon had given of Mrs. Lee’s charms, he could do no less than ask Senator Ratcliffe to take her in to dinner, which he did without delay. Either this, or the champagne, or some occult influence, had an extraordinary effect upon him. He appeared ten years younger than usual; his face was illuminated; his eyes glowed; he seemed bent on proving his kinship to the immortal Webster by rivalling his convivial powers. He dashed into the conversation; laughed, jested, and ridiculed; told stories in Yankee and Western dialect; gave sharp little sketches of amusing political experiences.
===Chinese===
施奈德库彭既已把李夫人的魅力描述得天花乱坠，他少不得请拉特克利夫参议员挽她入席，参议员也毫不迟延地照办了。也许是这个，也许是香槟，也许是某种秘不可测的影响，在他身上产生了非凡的效力。他看上去比平时年轻了十岁；满面生辉；双目炯炯；他似乎一心要与不朽的韦伯斯特比一比席间的豪兴，以证明自己与他一脉相承。他纵身跃入谈话；大笑、打趣、讥诮；用扬基土话和西部方言讲故事；三笔两笔，勾出几段有趣的政治阅历。

===Original===
“Never was more surprised in my life,” whispered Senator Krebs, of Pennsylvania, across the table to Schneidekoupon. “Hadn’t an idea that Ratcliffe was so entertaining.”

And Mr. Clinton, who sat by Madeleine on the other side, whispered low into her ear: “I am afraid, my dear Mrs. Lee, that you are responsible for this. He never talks so to the Senate.”
===Chinese===
“我这辈子没这么吃惊过，”宾夕法尼亚州的克雷布斯参议员（Senator Krebs）隔着桌子对施奈德库彭低语。“全想不到拉特克利夫竟这样有趣。”

而坐在马德琳另一侧的克林顿先生低声对她耳语：“亲爱的李夫人，恐怕这都得归咎于您。他在参议院可从来不这样说话。”

===Original===
Nay, he even rose to a higher flight, and told the story of President Lincoln’s deathbed with a degree of feeling that brought tears into their eyes. The other guests made no figure at all. The Speaker consumed his solitary duck and his lonely champagne in a corner without giving a sign. Even Mr. Gore, who was not wont to hide his light under any kind of extinguisher, made no attempt to claim the floor, and applauded with enthusiasm the conversation of his opposite neighbour. Ill-natured people might say that Mr. Gore saw in Senator Ratcliffe a possible Secretary of State; be this as it may, he certainly said to Mrs. Clinton, in an aside that was perfectly audible to everyone at the table: “How brilliant! what an original mind! what a sensation he would make abroad!” And it was quite true, apart from the mere momentary effect of dinner-table talk, that there was a certain bigness about the man; a keen practical sagacity; a bold freedom of self-assertion; a broad way of dealing with what he knew. Carrington was the only person at table who looked on with a perfectly cool head, and who criticised in a hostile spirit. Carrington’s impression of Ratcliffe was perhaps beginning to be warped by a shade of jealousy, for he was in a peculiarly bad temper this evening, and his irritation was not wholly concealed.
===Chinese===
不止如此，他甚至更上一层楼，讲起林肯总统临终的往事，那份深情叫众人眼里噙满了泪。其余宾客全然没了声色。议长独自在角落里受用他孤零零的烤鸭与寂寞的香槟，不动声色。连一向不肯把光藏在任何灯罩底下的戈尔先生，也全无抢占话头的企图，只热切地为对面邻座的谈吐鼓掌。刻薄的人也许会说，戈尔先生在拉特克利夫参议员身上看出了一位未来的国务卿；无论真相如何，他确实对克林顿夫人说了一句满桌人都听得一清二楚的旁白：“多么精彩！多么独到的头脑！他要到了国外，该掀起多大的轰动！”而平心而论，抛开席间谈兴的一时之效，此人也确有一种宏大的格局；敏锐而切实的睿智；敢于自任的豪放；处理分内所知时开阔的手段。满座唯有卡林顿一人头脑冷静地旁观，且怀着敌意加以批评。卡林顿对拉特克利夫的印象，或许已开始被一丝妒意扭歪了，因为这天晚上他脾气坏得出奇，恼怒也没有完全藏住。

===Original===
“If one only had any confidence in the man!” he muttered to French, who sat by him.

This unlucky remark set French to thinking how he could draw Ratcliffe out, and accordingly, with his usual happy manner, combining self-conceit and high principles, he began to attack the Senator with some “badinaige” on the delicate subject of Civil Service Reform, a subject almost as dangerous in political conversation at Washington as slavery itself in old days before the war. French was a reformer, and lost no occasion of impressing his views; but unluckily he was a very light weight, and his manner was a little ridiculous, so that even Mrs. Lee, who was herself a warm reformer, sometimes went over to the other side when he talked. No sooner had he now shot his little arrow at the Senator, than that astute man saw his opportunity, and promised himself the pleasure of administering to Mr. French punishment such as he knew would delight the company. Reformer as Mrs. Lee was, and a little alarmed at the roughness of Ratcliffe’s treatment, she could not blame the Prairie Giant, as she ought, who, after knocking poor French down, rolled him over and over in the mud.
===Chinese===
“要是能信得过这个人就好了！”他向坐在身旁的弗伦奇咕哝道。

这句倒霉话让弗伦奇动起脑筋，琢磨起怎样把拉特克利夫引出来；于是，带着他那副自负与高洁原则兼而有之的惯常妙态，他开始拿“打趣妙语”向参议员进攻，题目正是文官制度改革这块禁地——在华盛顿的政治谈话里，这题目的凶险，几乎不下于战前年月里的蓄奴制本身。弗伦奇是改革派，从不放过宣扬主张的机会；可惜他分量太轻，做派又有点可笑，以致连本身就是热烈改革派的李夫人，听他谈起来有时也要倒向对方那一边。他这支小箭刚朝参议员射出去，那位精明人立刻看准了机会，预先品味起教训弗伦奇先生的快感——他知道这一课必能博得满座欢喜。李夫人虽是改革派，见拉特克利夫下手狠辣也略感心惊，却未能像理当的那样去责怪草原巨人：他把可怜的弗伦奇打翻在地之后，又拖着他往泥里滚了一遭又一遭。

===Original===
“Are you financier enough, Mr. French, to know what are the most famous products of Connecticut?”

Mr. French modestly suggested that he thought its statesmen best answered that description.

“No, sir! even there you’re wrong. The showmen beat you on your own ground. But every child in the union knows that the most famous products of Connecticut are Yankee notions, nutmegs made of wood and clocks that won’t go. Now, your Civil Service Reform is just such another Yankee notion; it’s a wooden nutmeg; it’s a clock with a show case and sham works. And you know it! You are precisely the old-school Connecticut peddler. You have gone about peddling your wooden nutmegs until you have got yourself into Congress, and now you pull them out of your pockets and not only want us to take them at your own price, but you lecture us on our sins if we don’t. Well! we don’t mind your doing that at home. Abuse us as much as you like to your constituents. Get as many votes as you can. But don’t electioneer here, because we know you intimately, and we’ve all been a little in the wooden nutmeg business ourselves.”
===Chinese===
“弗伦奇先生，您这位理财大家，可知道康涅狄格最出名的物产是什么？”

弗伦奇先生谦逊地表示，他认为本州的政坛人物最当得起这个名目。

“不对，先生！就在这上头您也错了。耍把戏的班主在您自家的地盘上就压过了您。可合众国每个孩子都知道，康涅狄格最出名的物产是扬基小玩意儿：木头做的肉豆蔻，走不动的钟。您那文官制度改革，正是又一件扬基小玩意儿；它是一颗木头肉豆蔻；是一座装着玻璃罩、配着假机芯的钟。这您自己清楚！您正是个老派的康涅狄格货郎，走街串巷兜售您的木头肉豆蔻，一路把自己兜售进了国会，如今又从口袋里往外掏，不但要我们照您的开价买下，我们不买，您还要数落我们的罪过。好嘛！您在自家地面上这么做，我们不介意。您在选民面前爱怎么骂就怎么骂，能捞多少票就捞多少票。可别在这里拉票，因为我们知您的底细知得太深，况且木头肉豆蔻这门营生，我们自己也都沾过一点。”

===Original===
Senator Clinton and Senator Krebs chuckled high approval over this punishment of poor French, which was on the level of their idea of wit. They were all in the nutmeg business, as Ratcliffe said. The victim tried to make head against them; he protested that his nutmegs were genuine; he sold no goods that he did not guarantee; and that this particular article was actually guaranteed by the national conventions of both political parties.
===Chinese===
克林顿参议员和克雷布斯参议员咯咯连声，对可怜的弗伦奇领受的这顿教训大为称快——这正好合了他们对机智的见解水准。正如拉特克利夫所说，他们个个都在做肉豆蔻生意。受难者试图招架；他抗议说他的肉豆蔻是地道真货；他卖的货无不保真；何况这一件货色，两党的全国代表大会都实实在在作过保。

===Original===
“Then what you want, Mr. French, is a common school education. You need a little study of the alphabet. Or if you won’t believe me, ask my brother senators here what chance there is for your Reforms so long as the American citizen is what he is.”

“You’ll not get much comfort in my State, Mr. French,” growled the senator from Pennsylvania, with a sneer; “suppose you come and try.”

“Well, well!” said the benevolent Mr. Schuyler Clinton, gleaming benignantly through his gold spectacles; “don’t be too hard on French. He means well. Perhaps he’s not very wise, but he does good. I know more about it than any of you, and I don’t deny that the thing is all bad. Only, as Mr. Ratcliffe says, the difficulty is in the people, not in us. Go to work on them, French, and let us alone.”
===Chinese===
“那么您还缺的，弗伦奇先生，是一点公立小学的教育。您得稍微补一补字母。您要不信我，就问问在座这几位参议院同仁：只要美国公民还是眼下这副模样，您的改革还有几分指望。”

“在我的州里，您得不到多少安慰，弗伦奇先生，”宾夕法尼亚来的参议员冷笑着咆哮道；“不信您来试试。”

“好了，好了！”仁厚的斯凯勒·克林顿先生说着，透过金丝眼镜慈祥地闪着目光；“别对弗伦奇太狠。他用心是好的。人也许不甚聪明，事却做得好。这里面的情形我比你们谁都清楚，我也不否认这东西糟糕透顶。只是，正如拉特克利夫先生所说，难就难在民众，不在我们。弗伦奇，去对他们下功夫，别来缠我们。”

===Original===
French repented of his attack, and contented himself by muttering to Carrington: “What a set of damned old reprobates they are!”

“They are right, though, in one thing,” was Carrington’s reply: “their advice is good. Never ask one of them to reform anything; if you do, you will be reformed yourself.”
===Chinese===
弗伦奇悔了自己的进攻，只满足于向卡林顿咕哝：“他们这帮人，真是一群天打雷劈的老恶棍！”

“不过有一件事他们说得对，”卡林顿答道：“他们的忠告是金玉良言。千万别求他们哪一位去改革什么；您要真求了，挨改革的就得是您自己。”

===Original===
The dinner ended as brilliantly as it began, and Schneidekoupon was delighted with his success. He had made himself particularly agreeable to Sybil by confiding in her all his hopes and fears about the tariff and the finances. When the ladies left the table, Ratcliffe could not stay for a cigar; he must get back to his rooms, where he knew several men were waiting for him; he would take his leave of the ladies and hurry away. But when the gentlemen came up nearly an hour afterwards they found Ratcliffe still taking his leave of the ladies, who were delighted at his entertaining conversation; and when at last he really departed, he said to Mrs. Lee, as though it were quite a matter of course: “You are at home as usual tomorrow evening?” Madeleine smiled, bowed, and he went his way.
===Chinese===
宴席开场怎样辉煌，收场也怎样辉煌，施奈德库彭为自己的成功欢喜不迭。他把关税与财政上的全部希望和忧惧向西比尔和盘托出，博得她特别的好感。女士们离席后，拉特克利夫不能留下抽一支雪茄；他必须回寓所去，他知道有几位男士正在那里等他；他要向女士们道别，即刻赶路。可将近一个钟头后绅士们上楼来，却发现拉特克利夫还在向女士们道别——她们听他妙语连珠，正自心花怒放；待到他真正告辞而去时，他对李夫人说了一句，仿佛天经地义：“明天晚上您照旧在家？”马德琳含笑欠了欠身，他便去了。

===Original===
As the two sisters drove home that night, Madeleine was unusually silent. Sybil yawned convulsively and then apologized:

“Mr. Schneidekoupon is very nice and good-natured, but a whole evening of him goes a long way; and that horrid Senator Krebs would not say a word, and drank a great deal too much wine, though it couldn’t make him any more stupid than he is. I don’t think I care for senators.” Then, wearily, after a pause: “Well, Maude, I do hope you’ve got what you wanted. I’m sure you must have had politics enough. Haven’t you got to the heart of your great American mystery yet?”
===Chinese===
当晚姐妹俩乘车回家，马德琳异乎寻常地沉默。西比尔不由自主张大嘴打了个哈欠，随即赔了个不是：

“施奈德库彭先生人和气，脾气也好，可一整个晚上全是他一个人，就未免太熬人了；还有那个讨厌的克雷布斯参议员，一句话也不肯说，酒倒喝得太多——不过酒喝得再多，也不能叫他比本来更蠢。我想我是喜欢不起参议员来的。”停了停，又倦倦地说：“哎，莫德（Maude），我真希望你总算要到了想要的东西。政治你受的一定也够多了。你那伟大的美国之谜，还没挖到心么？”

===Original===
“Pretty near it, I think,” said Madeleine, half to herself.
===Chinese===
“我想，快挖到了。”马德琳半自言自语地说。

## IV / 四

===Original===
Sunday evening was stormy, and some enthusiasm was required to make one face its perils for the sake of society. Nevertheless, a few intimates made their appearance as usual at Mrs. Lee’s. The faithful Popoff was there, and Miss Dare also ran in to pass an hour with her dear Sybil; but as she passed the whole evening in a corner with Popoff, she must have been disappointed in her object. Carrington came, and Baron Jacobi. Schneidekoupon and his sister dined with Mrs. Lee, and remained after dinner, while Sybil and Julia Schneidekoupon compared conclusions about Washington society. The happy idea also occurred to Mr. Gore that, inasmuch as Mrs. Lee’s house was but a step from his hotel, he might as well take the chance of amusement there as the certainty of solitude in his rooms. Finally, Senator Ratcliffe duly made his appearance, and, having established himself with a cup of tea by Madeleine’s side, was soon left to enjoy a quiet talk with her, the rest of the party by common consent occupying themselves with each other. Under cover of the murmur of conversation in the room, Mr. Ratcliffe quickly became confidential.
===Chinese===
礼拜天晚上风雨大作，为了社交甘冒这般险恶，总得拿出几分热忱才行。尽管如此，几位至交仍照常到李夫人府上露面。忠心的波波夫来了；戴尔小姐也跑来，要跟她亲爱的西比尔消磨一个钟头；只是她一整晚都同波波夫窝在一个角落里，她的本意想必是落了空。卡林顿来了，雅各比男爵也来了。施奈德库彭兄妹在李夫人府上用了晚饭，饭后又留了下来，西比尔和朱莉娅·施奈德库彭就华盛顿社交界互通了结论。戈尔先生也灵机一动：既然李夫人的宅子离他下榻的旅馆不过一步之遥，与其回房独坐、把孤寂坐成定局，不如来这边碰一碰消遣的运气。末了，拉特克利夫参议员如期驾到，端着一杯茶在马德琳身边安顿下来，不久便由众人默契地留下，同她安享一场静谈，其余客人则心照不宣地各自周旋。借着满屋谈话的嗡嗡声遮掩，拉特克利夫先生很快就说起体己话来。

===Original===
“I came to suggest that, if you want to hear an interesting debate, you should come up to the Senate tomorrow. I am told that Garrard, of Louisiana, means to attack my last speech, and I shall probably in that case have to answer him. With you for a critic I shall speak better.”

“Am I such an amiable critic?” asked Madeleine.

“I never heard that amiable critics were the best,” said he; “justice is the soul of good criticism, and it is only justice that I ask and expect from you.”

“What good does this speaking do?” inquired she. “Are you any nearer the end of your difficulties by means of your speeches?”

“I hardly know yet. Just now we are in dead water; but this can’t last long. In fact, I am not afraid to tell you, though of course you will not repeat it to any human being, that we have taken measures to force an issue. Certain gentlemen, myself among the rest, have written letters meant for the President’s eye, though not addressed directly to him, and intended to draw out an expression of some sort that will show us what to expect.”
===Chinese===
“我来是想提议，您若想听一场有意思的辩论，明天不妨到参议院来。听说路易斯安那（Louisiana）的加拉德（Garrard）打算攻击我最近的那次演说，果真如此，我多半得应战。有您做批评家，我会讲得更好。”

“我是那样一位和蔼可亲的批评家么？”马德琳问。

“我从未听说和蔼的批评家就是最好的批评家，”他说；“公允是好的批评的灵魂，我向您要求、也指望从您那里得到的，唯有公允。”

“这些演说有什么用处？”她问道。“靠了这些演说，您离困难的尽头近了一些么？”

“我还说不准。眼下我们正处在死水之中；可这局面长不了。实话对您说吧——当然，您是不会向任何活人透露的——我们已经采取措施，来迫使摊牌。有几位先生，我也在其列，写了一些意在呈送总统御览的信，虽不是直接写给他的，目的在于引出他某种表态，好让我们知道该指望什么。”

===Original===
“Oh!” laughed Madeleine, “I knew about that a week ago.”

“About what?”

“About your letter to Sam Grimes, of North Bend.”

“What have you heard about my letter to Sam Grimes, of North Bend?” ejaculated Ratcliffe, a little abruptly.

“Oh, you do not know how admirably I have organised my secret service bureau,” said she. “Representative Cutter cross-questioned one of the Senate pages, and obliged him to confess that he had received from you a letter to be posted, which letter was addressed to Mr. Grimes, of North Bend.”

“And, of course, he told this to French, and French told you,” said Ratcliffe; “I see. If I had known this I would not have let French off so gently last night, for I prefer to tell you my own story without his embellishments. But it was my fault. I should not have trusted a page. Nothing is a secret here long. But one thing that Mr. Cutter did not find out was that several other gentlemen wrote letters at the same time, for the same purpose. Your friend, Mr. Clinton, wrote; Krebs wrote; and one or two members.”
===Chinese===
“哦！”马德琳笑道，“这事我一个礼拜前就知道了。”

“知道什么？”

“知道您写给北本德的萨姆·格莱姆斯的那封信。”

“关于我写给北本德的萨姆·格莱姆斯的信，您都听说了些什么？”拉特克利夫脱口问道，微微有些突兀。

“哦，您可不知道我的秘密谍报机关办得多么出色，”她说。“卡特众议员盘问了一名参议院听差，逼他招认：他从您手里接过一封待寄的信，那信正是寄给北本德的格莱姆斯先生的。”

“那么，自然是他告诉了弗伦奇，弗伦奇又告诉了您，”拉特克利夫说；“我明白了。早知如此，昨晚我就不会那样轻轻放过弗伦奇了，因为我宁愿亲口把我的故事讲给您听，不必添上他的枝叶。不过这是我的错。我不该信任一个听差。这里什么事都保不了多久的密。但有一件事卡特先生没能查出来：同时还有另外几位先生，为着同一目的也写了信。您那位朋友克林顿先生写了；克雷布斯写了；还有一两位议员。”

===Original===
“I suppose I must not ask what you said?”

“You may. We agreed that it was best to be very mild and conciliatory, and to urge the President only to give us some indication of his intentions, in order that we might not run counter to them. I drew a strong picture of the effect of the present situation on the party, and hinted that I had no personal wishes to gratify.”

“And what do you think will be the result?”

“I think we shall somehow manage to straighten things out,” said Ratcliffe. “The difficulty is only that the new President has little experience, and is suspicious. He thinks we shall intrigue to tie his hands, and he means to tie ours in advance. I don’t know him personally, but those who do, and who are fair judges, say that, though rather narrow and obstinate, he is honest enough, and will come round. I have no doubt I could settle it all with him in an hour’s talk, but it is out of the question for me to go to him unless I am asked, and to ask me to come would be itself a settlement.”
===Chinese===
“我想，信里写了什么，我是不该问的吧？”

“您可以问。我们商定，语气务须极其温和、极尽抚慰，只敦请总统给我们一点暗示，透一透他的意图，免得我们逆着它行事。我把当前局势对党的影响着力描画了一番，并暗示我自己并无个人的愿望要满足。”

“那您估计结果会怎样？”

“我想我们总会想法子把事情理顺的，”拉特克利夫说。“难只难在新总统阅历甚浅，又多疑。他以为我们要耍手腕捆住他的手脚，便打算抢先捆住我们的。我与他没有私交，但认识他而又肯秉公论断的人说，他虽然相当褊狭固执，人却够诚实，终究会回心转意。我不怀疑，同他谈上一个钟头，我便能把一切了结；可除非受召，我断无登门的道理，而请我前去，这本身便等于一种了结。”

===Original===
“What, then, is the danger you fear?”

“That he will offend all the important party leaders in order to conciliate unimportant ones, perhaps sentimental ones, like your friend French; that he will make foolish appointments without taking advice. By the way, have you seen French today?”

“No,” replied Madeleine; “I think he must be sore at your treatment of him last evening. You were very rude to him.”

“Not a bit,” said Ratcliffe; “these reformers need it. His attack on me was meant for a challenge. I saw it in his manner.”

“But is reform really so impossible as you describe it? Is it quite hopeless?”

“Reform such as he wants is utterly hopeless, and not even desirable.”
===Chinese===
“那么，您怕的危险是什么？”

“怕他为了安抚不重要的党魁——也许是感情用事的党魁，比如您那位朋友弗伦奇——把重要的党魁一股脑儿得罪光；怕他不听劝谏，做出愚蠢的任命。对了，您今天见着弗伦奇了么？”

“没有，”马德琳答道；“我想他准是为您昨晚对他的待遇憋着气呢。您对他太无礼了。”

“一点也不，”拉特克利夫说；“这些改革派正需要这个。他冲我来的那一手，本意是下战书。我从他的神气里看出来了。”

“可是改革真像您说的那样无望么？当真毫无指望了么？”

“像他想要的那种改革，全然无望，甚至不值得要。”

===Original===
Mrs. Lee, with much earnestness of manner, still pressed her question: “Surely something can be done to check corruption. Are we forever to be at the mercy of thieves and ruffians? Is a respectable government impossible in a democracy?”

Her warmth attracted Jacobi’s attention, and he spoke across the room. “What is that you say, Mrs. Lee? What is it about corruption?”

All the gentlemen began to listen and gather about them.

“I am asking Senator Ratcliffe,” said she, “what is to become of us if corruption is allowed to go unchecked.”

“And may I venture to ask permission to hear Mr. Ratcliffe’s reply?” asked the baron.

“My reply,” said Ratcliffe, “is that no representative government can long be much better or much worse than the society it represents. Purify society and you purify the government. But try to purify the government artificially and you only aggravate failure.”
===Chinese===
李夫人神态恳切，仍旧追问不休：“总该有法子遏止贪腐吧。难道我们要永远任凭窃贼与恶棍摆布么？民主政体之下，一个体面的政府就不可能么？”

她的热烈引来了雅各比的注意，他隔着屋子搭话：“您说什么呢，李夫人？说的是贪腐么？”

绅士们全都竖起耳朵，朝她们围拢过来。

“我正问拉特克利夫参议员，”她说，“倘若任凭贪腐横行不加遏止，我们会落得什么下场。”

“那我可否斗胆请求恩准，聆听拉特克利夫先生的高见？”男爵问。

“我的回答是，”拉特克利夫说，“任何代议制政府，都不能长久地比它所代表的社会好得多，也不能坏得多。净化社会，则政府自净。可若想人为地去净化政府，只会叫失败愈演愈烈。”

===Original===
“A very statesmanlike reply,” said Baron Jacobi, with a formal bow, but his tone had a shade of mockery. Carrington, who had listened with a darkening face, suddenly turned to the baron and asked him what conclusion he drew from the reply.

“Ah!” exclaimed the baron, with his wickedest leer, “what for is my conclusion good? You Americans believe yourselves to be excepted from the operation of general laws. You care not for experience. I have lived seventy-five years, and all that time in the midst of corruption. I am corrupt myself, only I do have courage to proclaim it, and you others have it not. Rome, Paris, Vienna, Petersburg, London, all are corrupt; only Washington is pure! Well, I declare to you that in all my experience I have found no society which has had elements of corruption like the United States. The children in the street are corrupt, and know how to cheat me. The cities are all corrupt, and also the towns and the counties and the States’ legislatures and the judges. Everywhere men betray trusts both public and private, steal money, run away with public funds. Only in the Senate men take no money. And you gentlemen in the Senate very well declare that your great United States, which is the head of the civilized world, can never learn anything from the example of corrupt Europe. You are right﻿—quite right! The great United States needs not an example. I do much regret that I have not yet one hundred years to live. If I could then come back to this city, I should find myself very content﻿—much more than now. I am always content where there is much corruption, and *ma parole d’honneur!*” broke out the old man with fire and gesture, “the United States will then be more corrupt than Rome under Caligula; more corrupt than the Church under Leo X; more corrupt than France under the Regent!”
===Chinese===
“好一个政治家的答复，”雅各比男爵说着，规规矩矩鞠了一躬，语调里却带一分讥诮。卡林顿一直沉着脸听着，这时突然转向男爵，问他从这答复里得出什么结论。

“啊！”男爵喊道，摆出他最歹毒的斜睨，“我的结论有个什么用处？你们美国人相信自己是普遍规律之外的特例。你们不在乎经验。我活了七十五个年头，一直泡在贪腐中间。我自己就是个贪腐之人，只不过我有胆量公然承认，你们诸位没有罢了。罗马、巴黎、维也纳、彼得堡、伦敦，处处贪腐；唯独华盛顿是净土！好哇，我向您声明：凭我一生的阅历，我从未见过哪个社会，其贪腐的元素堪与合众国相比。街上的孩童都是贪腐的，还懂得耍花招骗我。城市无不贪腐，村镇贪腐，各县贪腐，各州议会贪腐，法官也贪腐。到处有人辜负公私两面的信托，偷钱，卷了公款潜逃。唯独参议院里，人不拿钱。而你们这些参议院里的先生们，还振振有词地宣告：你们伟大的合众国——文明世界的头脑——永远休想从贪腐的欧洲身上学到半点教训。你们说得对——对极了！伟大的合众国不需要教训。我只深深遗憾，自己没能再活上一百年。到那时我若能回到这座城市，我准会发现自己称心得很——比现在称心得多。哪里贪腐繁盛，我便在哪里自在，我以名誉担保（ma parole d’honneur）！”老人带着火气与手势脱口喊道，“到那时，合众国将比卡利古拉（Caligula）治下的罗马更贪腐；比利奥十世（Leo X）治下的教会更贪腐；比摄政王治下的法国更贪腐！”

===Original===
As the baron closed his little harangue, which he delivered directly at the senator sitting underneath him, he had the satisfaction to see that everyone was silent and listening with deep attention. He seemed to enjoy annoying the senator, and he had the satisfaction of seeing that the senator was visibly annoyed. Ratcliffe looked sternly at the baron and said, with some curtness, that he saw no reason to accept such conclusions. Conversation flagged, and all except the baron were relieved when Sybil, at Schneidekoupon’s request, sat down at the piano to sing what she called a hymn. So soon as the song was over, Ratcliffe, who seemed to have been curiously thrown off his balance by Jacobi’s harangue, pleaded urgent duties at his rooms, and retired. The others soon afterwards went off in a body, leaving only Carrington and Gore, who had seated himself by Madeleine, and was at once dragged by her into a discussion of the subject which perplexed her, and for the moment threw over her mind a net of irresistible fascination.
===Chinese===
男爵这通小小的高谈，是冲着坐在他下首的参议员直接发出的；收梢时，他满意地看到满座寂然，人人凝神细听。他似乎以惹恼参议员为乐，而且满意地看到参议员显然被惹恼了。拉特克利夫严厉地望了男爵一眼，带着几分生硬说，他不认为有什么理由接受这样的结论。谈话萎了下去；西比尔应施奈德库彭之请在钢琴前坐下，唱起她所谓的圣歌时，除男爵以外人人都松了口气。歌一唱完，拉特克利夫便推说寓所有要务亟待料理，退了出去——雅各比那通高谈似乎离奇地叫他失了平衡。其余的人随后也成群结伙地散去，只留下卡林顿和戈尔；后者已在马德琳身边坐下，立刻被她拉进一场讨论，讨论的正是那个令她困惑的题目——此刻，这题目在她心头罩下了一张无可抗拒的迷网。

===Original===
“The baron discomfited the senator,” said Gore, with a certain hesitation. “Why did Ratcliffe let himself be trampled upon in that manner?”

“I wish you would explain why,” replied Mrs. Lee; “tell me, Mr. Gore﻿—you who represent cultivation and literary taste hereabouts﻿—please tell me what to think about Baron Jacobi’s speech. Who and what is to be believed? Mr. Ratcliffe seems honest and wise. Is he a corruptionist? He believes in the people, or says he does. Is he telling the truth or not?”

Gore was too experienced in politics to be caught in such a trap as this. He evaded the question. “Mr. Ratcliffe has a practical piece of work to do; his business is to make laws and advise the President; he does it extremely well. We have no other equally good practical politician; it is unfair to require him to be a crusader besides.”

“No!” interposed Carrington, curtly; “but he need not obstruct crusades. He need not talk virtue and oppose the punishment of vice.”

“He is a shrewd practical politician,” replied Gore, “and he feels first the weak side of any proposed political tactics.”
===Chinese===
“男爵折了参议员的锐气，”戈尔带着几分迟疑说。“拉特克利夫为什么甘愿让人那样践踏呢？”

“我倒巴不得您解释解释为什么，”李夫人答道；“告诉我，戈尔先生——您在此地代表着教养与文学趣味——请告诉我，对雅各比男爵那篇话该怎么想。该相信谁、相信什么？拉特克利夫先生看上去又诚实又明智。他是个贪腐分子么？他信仰人民，或者口称信仰。他到底说的是真话不是？”

戈尔在政治上老于此道，岂会落入这样的圈套。他把问题闪开了。“拉特克利夫先生手头有一件实事要做；他的本分是立法、是给总统献策；这件实事他做得极其出色。同样出色的实干政治家，我们再找不出第二个；要求他兼任十字军，未免不公。”

“是不该！”卡林顿简截地插话；“可他也不必阻挠十字军。他不必满口道德，却反对惩治罪恶。”

“他是位精明的实干政治家，”戈尔答道，“任何政治方略一经提出，他总是头一个摸到它的软肋。”

===Original===
With a sigh of despair Madeleine went on: “Who, then, is right? How *can* we all be right? Half of our wise men declare that the world is going straight to perdition; the other half that it is fast becoming perfect. Both cannot be right. There is only one thing in life,” she went on, laughing, “that I must and will have before I die. I must know whether America is right or wrong. Just now this question is a very practical one, for I really want to know whether to believe in Mr. Ratcliffe. If I throw him overboard, everything must go, for he is only a specimen.”

“Why not believe in Mr. Ratcliffe?” said Gore; “I believe in him myself, and am not afraid to say so.”

Carrington, to whom Ratcliffe now began to represent the spirit of evil, interposed here, and observed that he imagined Mr. Gore had other guides besides, and steadier ones than Ratcliffe, to believe in; while Madeleine, with a certain feminine perspicacity, struck at a much weaker point in Mr. Gore’s armour, and asked point-blank whether he believed also in what Ratcliffe represented: “Do you yourself think democracy the best government, and universal suffrage a success?”

Mr. Gore saw himself pinned to the wall, and he turned at bay with almost the energy of despair:
===Chinese===
马德琳绝望地叹了口气，接着说：“那么谁对？我们又怎么可能都对？我们的智者，一半宣告世界正一路直堕毁灭，另一半宣告它正在飞速臻于至善。两半不可能都对。人这一生只有一件事，”她笑着继续说，“是我临死之前必须弄到、也一定要弄到手的。我必须知道美国是对的还是错的。眼下这个问题非常实际，因为我实在想要知道，该不该相信拉特克利夫先生。我若把他抛下船，一切就都得跟着下船，因为他不过是一个标本。”

“为什么不信拉特克利夫先生呢？”戈尔说；“我自己就信他，而且不怕说出来。”

在卡林顿眼里，拉特克利夫如今开始化身恶的精灵；他这时插进来，说据他猜想，戈尔先生除了拉特克利夫之外，还有别的指南可信，而且比拉特克利夫更稳当；马德琳则带着一种女性特有的敏锐，直取戈尔先生甲胄上薄弱得多的部位，直截了当问他，是否也相信拉特克利夫所代表的东西：“您自己是否认为民主是最好的政体，普选是一场成功？”

戈尔先生眼看自己被逼到了墙角，便背水一战，几乎使出了绝望的劲头：

===Original===
“These are matters about which I rarely talk in society; they are like the doctrine of a personal God; of a future life; of revealed religion; subjects which one naturally reserves for private reflection. But since you ask for my political creed, you shall have it. I only condition that it shall be for you alone, never to be repeated or quoted as mine. I believe in democracy. I accept it. I will faithfully serve and defend it. I believe in it because it appears to me the inevitable consequence of what has gone before it. Democracy asserts the fact that the masses are now raised to a higher intelligence than formerly. All our civilisation aims at this mark. We want to do what we can to help it. I myself want to see the result. I grant it is an experiment, but it is the only direction society can take that is worth its taking; the only conception of its duty large enough to satisfy its instincts; the only result that is worth an effort or a risk. Every other possible step is backward, and I do not care to repeat the past. I am glad to see society grapple with issues in which no one can afford to be neutral.”

“And supposing your experiment fails,” said Mrs. Lee; “suppose society destroys itself with universal suffrage, corruption, and communism.”
===Chinese===
“这些事我极少在社交场合谈论；它们好比人格神的教义、来生的教义、天启宗教的教义，是一个人自然留给自己独自咀嚼的题目。但既然您要我的政治信条，那就拿去。我只有一个条件：它只给您一个人，永远不得重复转述，也不得引为我说的话。我信仰民主。我接受它。我将忠心地侍奉它、捍卫它。我信仰它，因为在我看来，它是此前一切演变的必然结果。民主断言这样一个事实：民众如今已被提升到高于以往的智识。我们全部的文明都以这个目标为鹄的。我们愿意竭尽所能助它一臂。我本人想亲眼看到结果。我承认这是一场实验，但它是社会唯一值得迈出的方向；是唯一大到足以安放其本能的使命构想；是唯一值得付出一番努力、担一回风险的结局。其余一切可能的步子都是倒退，而我不愿意重蹈过去。看到社会扭住这些不容任何人袖手旁观的问题，我很高兴。”

“可万一您的实验失败了呢，”李夫人说；“万一社会让普选、贪腐和共产主义毁掉了自己呢？”

===Original===
“I wish, Mrs. Lee, you would visit the Observatory with me some evening, and look at Sirius. Did you ever make the acquaintance of a fixed star? I believe astronomers reckon about twenty millions of them in sight, and an infinite possibility of invisible millions, each one of which is a sun, like ours, and may have satellites like our planet. Suppose you see one of these fixed stars suddenly increase in brightness, and are told that a satellite has fallen into it and is burning up, its career finished, its capacities exhausted? Curious, is it not; but what does it matter? Just as much as the burning up of a moth at your candle.”

Madeleine shuddered a little. “I cannot get to the height of your philosophy,” said she. “You are wandering among the infinites, and I am finite.”

“Not at all! But I have faith; not perhaps in the old dogmas, but in the new ones; faith in human nature; faith in science; faith in the survival of the fittest. Let us be true to our time, Mrs. Lee! If our age is to be beaten, let us die in the ranks. If it is to be victorious, let us be first to lead the column. Anyway, let us not be skulkers or grumblers. There! have I repeated my catechism correctly? You would have it! Now oblige me by forgetting it. I should lose my character at home if it got out. Good night!”
===Chinese===
“李夫人，我但愿您哪个晚上同我去一趟天文台，看看天狼星（Sirius）。您可曾结识过一颗恒星？据信天文学家估计，视野之内约有两千万颗，看不见的还有无穷个几百万的可能；每一颗都是一个太阳，同我们的太阳一样，说不定也带着我们这颗行星一般的卫星。设想您眼见其中一颗恒星骤然大放光明，又有人告诉您：一颗卫星坠入了它，正在焚毁，它的生涯完结了，它的潜能耗竭了——您觉得如何？离奇吧；可这又有什么要紧？要紧的程度，恰如一只飞蛾扑进您的蜡烛里烧掉。”

马德琳轻轻打了个寒噤。“我登不上您哲学的高度，”她说。“您在无穷之间漫游，而我是有穷的。”

“哪里的话！不过我有信仰；或许不在旧教条里，而在新教条里；信人性；信科学；信适者生存。让我们忠于自己的时代吧，李夫人！倘若我们的时代注定败北，就让我们死在队列里。倘若它注定得胜，就让我们第一个执掌前导。无论如何，别做逃兵，别做怨夫。瞧！我的教义问答背得还准么？是您非要听的！现在劳驾把它忘掉。这话若是传出去，我在家乡可就要名声扫地了。晚安！”

===Original===
Mrs. Lee duly appeared at the Capitol the next day, as she could not but do after Senator Ratcliffe’s pointed request. She went alone, for Sybil had positively refused to go near the Capitol again, and Madeleine thought that on the whole this was not an occasion for enrolling Carrington in her service. But Ratcliffe did not speak. The debate was unexpectedly postponed. He joined Mrs. Lee in the gallery, however, sat with her as long as she would allow, and became still more confidential, telling her that he had received the expected reply from Grimes, of North Bend, and that it had enclosed a letter written by the President-elect to Mr. Grimes in regard to the advances made by Mr. Ratcliffe and his friends.

“It is not a handsome letter,” said he; “indeed, a part of it is positively insulting. I would like to read you one extract from it, and hear your opinion as to how it should be treated.” Taking the letter from his pocket, he sought out the passage, and read as follows: “ ‘I cannot lose sight, too, of the consideration that these three Senators’ (he means Clinton, Krebs, and me) ‘are popularly considered to be the most influential members of that so-called senatorial ring, which has acquired such general notoriety. While I shall always receive their communications with all due respect, I must continue to exercise complete freedom of action in consulting other political advisers as well as these, and I must in all cases make it my first object to follow the wishes of the people, not always most truly represented by their nominal representatives.’ What say you to that precious piece of presidential manners?”
===Chinese===
第二天，李夫人如期出现在国会大厦——拉特克利夫参议员既有那番恳切的邀请，她不去不行。她独自前往，因为西比尔斩钉截铁地拒绝再走近国会大厦一步，而马德琳觉得，总的说来，这一回不是把卡林顿收编入役的场合。可拉特克利夫没有登台。辩论意外延期了。他却到旁听席上与李夫人会合，在她容许的时限里一直陪坐，愈发地推心置腹，告诉她：北本德的格莱姆斯那边，他已收到那封意料之中的回信，信里还附着当选总统写给格莱姆斯先生的一封信，谈的正是拉特克利夫先生及其朋友们的那番试探。

“这可不是一封体面的信，”他说；“说实话，有一部分简直是侮辱。我想给您念其中一段，听听您的意见，该当如何处置。”他从口袋里取出信，找出那一段，念道：“‘此外我也不能无视这样一层考虑：这三位参议员’（他指的是克林顿、克雷布斯和我）‘世人公认为那个所谓参议员帮中最有权势的成员，该帮早已臭名远扬。他们的来函我自当始终以应有之礼相待，但在咨询政治顾问一事上，除他们之外，我还必须继续保持咨询他人的完全行动自由；且无论何时，我都必须把顺从人民的意愿当作第一要务——人民的意愿，并不总是被他们名义上的代表最真切地体现的。’这份总统风度的珍品，您有何高见？”

===Original===
“At least I like his courage,” said Mrs. Lee.

“Courage is one thing; common sense is another. This letter is a studied insult. He has knocked me off the track once. He means to do it again. It is a declaration of war. What ought I to do?”

“Whatever is most for the public good,” said Madeleine, gravely.

Ratcliffe looked into her face with such undisguised delight﻿—there was so little possibility of mistaking or ignoring the expression of his eyes, that she shrank back with a certain shock. She was not prepared for so open a demonstration. He hardened his features at once, and went on:

“But what *is* most for the public good?”

“That you know better than I,” said Madeleine; “only one thing is clear to me. If you let yourself be ruled by your private feelings, you will make a greater mistake than he. Now I must go, for I have visits to make. The next time I come, Mr. Ratcliffe, you must keep your word better.”
===Chinese===
“至少我喜欢他的胆气，”李夫人说。

“胆气是一回事，常识是另一回事。这封信是处心积虑的侮辱。他已经把我从轨道上掀下去过一回。他还打算再来。这是一纸宣战。我该怎么办？”

“做最合乎公益的事，”马德琳庄重地说。

拉特克利夫望着她的脸，那欢喜毫不掩饰——他眼中那副神情，实在没有多少误认或无视的余地，她微微一惊，向后缩了缩。这样公开的表露，她没有防备。他立即绷紧了面容，接着说：

“可什么才最合乎公益呢？”

“这您比我清楚，”马德琳说；“只有一件事我看得分明。您若让私情牵着走，您犯的错就会比他还大。现在我得走了，还要拜客去。下回我来的时候，拉特克利夫先生，您的信用可得守得比这回好些。”

===Original===
When they next met, Ratcliffe read to her a part of his reply to Mr. Grimes, which ran thus: “It is the lot of every party leader to suffer from attacks and to commit errors. It is true, as the President says, that I have been no exception to this law. Believing as I do that great results can only be accomplished by great parties, I have uniformly yielded my own personal opinions where they have failed to obtain general assent. I shall continue to follow this course, and the President may with perfect confidence count upon my disinterested support of all party measures, even though I may not be consulted in originating them.”

Mrs. Lee listened attentively, and then said: “Have you never refused to go with your party?”

“Never!” was Ratcliffe’s firm reply.

Madeleine still more thoughtfully inquired again: “Is nothing more powerful than party allegiance?”

“Nothing, except national allegiance,” replied Ratcliffe, still more firmly.
===Chinese===
两人再度会面时，拉特克利夫给她念了他致格莱姆斯先生回信中的一部分，信里这样写道：“凡党魁者，受攻击、犯过失，皆是命定的分内事。诚如总统所言，我在这条定律面前也非例外。我既深信伟大的成果唯有靠大党方能成就，故凡个人意见未能获得普遍赞同时，我一概让它屈从。我将继续奉行此道，总统尽可完全放心地指望我对党的各项措施给予无私的支持，纵然那些措施的肇始未必征询于我。”

李夫人凝神听完，说道：“您从来没有拒绝过随党行动么？”

“从来没有！”拉特克利夫斩钉截铁地答道。

马德琳愈发沉思，又问：“没有什么比效忠党更强有力的了么？”

“没有了，除了效忠国家，”拉特克利夫答道，语气愈发坚决。

## V / 五

===Original===
To tie a prominent statesman to her train and to lead him about like a tame bear, is for a young and vivacious woman a more certain amusement than to tie herself to him and to be dragged about like an Indian squaw. This fact was Madeleine Lee’s first great political discovery in Washington, and it was worth to her all the German philosophy she had ever read, with even a complete edition of Herbert Spencer’s works into the bargain. There could be no doubt that the honours and dignities of a public career were no fair consideration for its pains. She made a little daily task for herself of reading in succession the lives and letters of the American Presidents, and of their wives, when she could find that there was a trace of the latter’s existence. What a melancholy spectacle it was, from George Washington down to the last incumbent; what vexations, what disappointments, what grievous mistakes, what very objectionable manners! Not one of them, who had aimed at high purpose, but had been thwarted, beaten, and habitually insulted! What a gloom lay on the features of those famous chieftains, Calhoun, Clay, and Webster; what varied expression of defeat and unsatisfied desire; what a sense of self-importance and senatorial magniloquence; what a craving for flattery; what despair at the sentence of fate! And what did they amount to, after all?

They were practical men, these! they had no great problems of thought to settle, no questions that rose above the ordinary rules of common morals and homely duty. How they had managed to befog the subject! What elaborate show-structures they had built up, with no result but to obscure the horizon! Would not the country have done better without them? Could it have done worse? What deeper abyss could have opened under the nation’s feet, than that to whose verge they brought it?

Madeleine’s mind wearied with the monotony of the story. She discussed the subject with Ratcliffe, who told her frankly that the pleasure of politics lay in the possession of power. He agreed that the country would do very well without him. “But here I am,” said he, “and here I mean to stay.” He had very little sympathy for thin moralising, and a statesmanlike contempt for philosophical politics. He loved power, and he meant to be President. That was enough.
===Chinese===
把一位显赫的政治家拴在自己的裙裾之后，牵着他走来走去，像牵一头驯顺的熊，对于一个年轻活泼的女人来说，总比把自己拴在他身上、像印第安婆娘似的被他拖来拖去，更是十拿九稳的乐事。这个道理，是马德琳·李在华盛顿的第一桩重大政治发现，在她看来，抵得过她读过的全部德国哲学，外加一部赫伯特·斯宾塞全集，也还绰绰有余。公共生涯的那点荣誉与显贵，断然抵不过它带来的辛劳，这一点再无疑问。她给自己定下一桩小小的日课：依次研读历届美国总统的传记与书信，连总统夫人们的也读——只要寻得到她们存在过的些微痕迹。那是一幅何等凄凉的景象，从乔治·华盛顿一路数到最近这一任；多少恼恨，多少失望，多少痛心的过失，多少叫人不敢恭维的做派！凡志存高远者，无一不曾受挫、被打压、被习以为常地羞辱！卡尔霍恩、克莱、韦伯斯特这些著名的党魁宿将，脸上笼罩着何等的阴郁；那上面有形形色色的败绩与未餍的欲望，有妄自尊大与参议院式的高腔大调，有对谀辞的如饥似渴，有对命运判词的绝望！而到头来，他们又算得了什么呢？

这可都是些实干家！他们没有什么伟大的思想课题要解决，没有什么问题高出寻常道德与本分家常的规矩。可他们把事情搅得多么混沌！他们筑起了何等精工的门面楼台，结果无非把地平线遮得严严实实！没有他们，国家岂不会更好？难道还能更坏么？他们既已把国家领到了深渊的边沿，还有什么更深的深渊，能在国族的脚下豁然张开？

这千篇一律的故事，听得马德琳心生倦意。她同拉特克利夫谈起这个题目，他直言不讳地告诉她：政治的乐趣，在于权力的持有。他也承认，没有他，国家会治理得很好。“可我既已在此，”他说，“便打算在此待下去。”他对单薄的道德说教几乎全无同情，对哲学化的政治则抱着一股政治家式的轻蔑。他爱权力，并且打算当总统。这就够了。

===Original===
Sometimes the tragic and sometimes the comic side was uppermost in her mind, and sometimes she did not herself know whether to cry or to laugh. Washington more than any other city in the world swarms with simple-minded exhibitions of human nature; men and women curiously out of place, whom it would be cruel to ridicule and ridiculous to weep over. The sadder exhibitions are fortunately seldom seen by respectable people; only the little social accidents come under their eyes. One evening Mrs. Lee went to the President’s first evening reception. As Sybil flatly refused to face the crowd, and Carrington mildly said that he feared he was not sufficiently reconstructed to appear at home in that august presence, Mrs. Lee accepted Mr. French for an escort, and walked across the Square with him to join the throng that was pouring into the doors of the White House. They took their places in the line of citizens and were at last able to enter the reception-room. There Madeleine found herself before two seemingly mechanical figures, which might be wood or wax, for any sign they showed of life. These two figures were the President and his wife; they stood stiff and awkward by the door, both their faces stripped of every sign of intelligence, while the right hands of both extended themselves to the column of visitors with the mechanical action of toy dolls. Mrs. Lee for a moment began to laugh, but the laugh died on her lips. To the President and his wife this was clearly no laughing matter. There they stood, automata, representatives of the society which streamed past them. Madeleine seized Mr. French by the arm.

“Take me somewhere at once,” said she, “where I can look at it. Here! in the corner. I had no conception how shocking it was!”

Mr. French supposed she was thinking of the queer-looking men and women who were swarming through the rooms, and he made, after his own delicate notion of humour, some uncouth jests on those who passed by. Mrs. Lee, however, was in no humour to explain or even to listen. She stopped him short:﻿—

“There, Mr. French! Now go away and leave me. I want to be alone for half an hour. Please come for me then.” And there she stood, with her eyes fixed on the President and his wife, while the endless stream of humanity passed them, shaking hands.
===Chinese===
有时是悲的一面、有时是喜的一面在她心头占了上风，有时连她自己也拿不准该哭还是该笑。华盛顿比世上任何城市都更充斥着质朴赤裸的人性展演；男男女女身处奇妙的错位之中，嘲笑他们未免残忍，为他们垂泪又未免可笑。那些更凄凉的展演，幸而体面人难得一见；入得了他们眼的，只是些小小的社交事故。一天晚上，李夫人去出席总统的首场晚间招待会。西比尔斩钉截铁地不肯去面对那群人；卡林顿则婉言说，恐怕自己还没有经过充分的“重建”，不足以在那般庄严的驾前安然自处；李夫人便请弗伦奇先生充当护卫，同他穿过广场，汇入正涌进白宫大门的人流。他们排进公民的长队，终于进了接待厅。在那里，马德琳发现自己面对着两具仿佛机械的人形，依它们所显的生命迹象来看，不是木头，便是蜡。这两个人形便是总统和总统夫人；他们僵直而局促地立在门边，两张脸上剥尽了一切智慧的痕迹，两人的右手则以玩具娃娃般的机械动作，伸向鱼贯而至的来客。李夫人一时竟笑了起来，可笑意到了唇边便消散了。对总统和总统夫人来说，这显然绝非可笑之事。他们立在那里，两具自动木偶，代表着从他们面前川流而过的社会。马德琳一把抓住弗伦奇先生的手臂。

“马上带我到什么地方去，”她说，“好让我看着它。这儿！就这个角落。我全然想不到这景象竟这么骇人！”

弗伦奇先生以为她想着的是那些涌满各厅、模样古怪的男男女女，便循着他自己那套精致的幽默观，对过往的人物打了几句粗野的诨语。李夫人却全无心情解释，甚至连听也不要听。她径直截断了他：

“得了，弗伦奇先生！现在您走开吧，别管我。我要独自待上半个钟头。到时候再来接我。”她便立在那里，眼睛定在总统和总统夫人身上，任那无尽的人流从他们面前经过，一一握手。

===Original===
What a strange and solemn spectacle it was, and how the deadly fascination of it burned the image in upon her mind! What a horrid warning to ambition! And in all that crowd there was no one besides herself who felt the mockery of this exhibition. To all the others this task was a regular part of the President’s duty, and there was nothing ridiculous about it. They thought it a democratic institution, this droll aping of monarchical forms. To them the deadly dullness of the show was as natural and proper as ever to the courtiers of the Philips and Charleses seemed the ceremonies of the Escorial. To her it had the effect of a nightmare, or of an opium-eater’s vision. She felt a sudden conviction that this was to be the end of American society; its realisation and dream at once. She groaned in spirit.

“Yes! at last I have reached the end! We shall grow to be wax images, and our talk will be like the squeaking of toy dolls. We shall all wander round and round the earth and shake hands. No one will have any object in this world, and there will be no other. It is worse than anything in the *Inferno*. What an awful vision of eternity!”
===Chinese===
那是何等离奇而肃穆的景象，它那致命的蛊惑又把这幅影像何等深刻地烙进她的脑海！对野心是何等可怖的警戒！而满场众人当中，除她之外，再无一人感到这场展演的嘲弄。对其余所有人来说，这差事是总统职分的常规一环，毫无可笑之处。他们把这套对君主典仪的滑稽仿拟，当作一项民主制度。在他们看来，这场展演那致命的乏味，正如埃斯科里亚尔（Escorial）的种种典礼之于菲利普诸王与查理诸王的朝臣，从来自然而又合体。而在她，这却像一场梦魇，像服食鸦片者的幻象。她蓦然确信：这将是美国社会的尽头；是它的实现，也是它的梦，一时并至。她在心里呻吟。

“是的！我终于抵达尽头了！我们将渐渐长成蜡像，我们的谈吐将如玩具娃娃的吱吱声。我们都将绕着地球一圈又一圈地游荡、握手。谁在这个世界上都将不再有任何目标，而除这世界外，又再没有别的世界了。这比《地狱篇》（Inferno）里的任何一处还糟。何等可怕的永恒图景！”

===Original===
Suddenly, as through a mist, she saw the melancholy face of Lord Skye approaching. He came to her side, and his voice recalled her to reality.

“Does it amuse you, this sort of thing?” he asked in a vague way.

“We take our amusement sadly, after the manner of our people,” she replied; “but it certainly interests me.”

They stood for a time in silence, watching the slowly eddying dance of Democracy, until he resumed:
===Chinese===
蓦地，仿佛透过一层雾，她看见斯凯勋爵那张忧郁的脸正在靠近。他走到她身旁，他的声音把她唤回了现实。

“这种事情让您觉得有趣么？”他含含糊糊地问。

“我们取乐取得很是沉痛，这是我们民族的路数，”她答道；“不过它确乎让我感兴趣。”

两人默默立了一阵，望着民主政治这场缓缓回旋的舞蹈，直到他又开口：

===Original===
“Whom do you take that man to be﻿—the long, lean one, with a long woman on each arm?”

“That man,” she replied, “I take to be a Washington department-clerk, or perhaps a member of Congress from Iowa, with a wife and wife’s sister. Do they shock your nobility?”

He looked at her with comical resignation. “You mean to tell me that they are quite as good as dowager-countesses. I grant it. My aristocratic spirit is broken, Mrs. Lee. I will even ask them to dinner if you bid me, and if you will come to meet them. But the last time I asked a member of Congress to dine, he sent me back a note in pencil on my own envelope that he would bring two of his friends with him, very respectable constituents from Yahoo city, or some such place; nature’s noblemen, he said.”

“You should have welcomed them.”
===Chinese===
“您猜那个人是谁——那个瘦长个子、两臂各挽着一个高挑女人的？”

“那个人么，”她答道，“我猜是华盛顿某个部的科员，要不就是艾奥瓦（Iowa）来的国会议员，带着太太和小姨子。他们可惊犯了您的贵族威严？”

他带着滑稽的听天由命神气望着她。“您这是要告诉我，他们同老伯爵夫人一样金贵。我认了。我的贵族精神已经折了，李夫人。只要您吩咐，只要您肯来同他们见面，我甚至可以请他们来赴宴。可上一回我请一位国会议员来吃饭，他用铅笔在我自己的信封上写了张字条退回来，说他要带两位朋友同来——都是雅虎城（Yahoo city）还是什么地方的非常体面的选民；天生的贵族，他说。”

“您本该欢迎他们的。”

===Original===
“I did. I wanted to see two of nature’s noblemen, and I knew they would probably be pleasanter company than their representative. They came; very respectable persons, one with a blue necktie, the other with a red one: both had diamond pins in their shirts, and were carefully brushed in respect to their hair. They said nothing, ate little, drank less, and were much better behaved than I am. When they went away, they unanimously asked me to stay with them when I visited Yahoo city.”

“You will not want guests if you always do that.”

“I don’t know. I think it was pure ignorance on their part. They knew no better, and they seemed modest enough. My only complaint was that I could get nothing out of them. I wonder whether their wives would have been more amusing.”

“Would they be so in England, Lord Skye?”
===Chinese===
“我欢迎了。我正想见识两位天生的贵族，而且我知道，他们多半比他们的代表更好作伴。他们来了；都是很体面的人物，一个系蓝领结，一个系红领结：衬衫上都别着钻石饰针，头发也梳理得一丝不苟。他们一言不发，吃得少，喝得更少，举止比我规矩得多。临走的时候，他们异口同声地请我日后到雅虎城时住到他们家去。”

“您要是总这样办，就不愁没有客人了。”

“谁知道呢。我想那纯粹是出于无知。他们不懂规矩，人倒似乎相当谦和。我唯一的抱怨，是从他们嘴里掏不出一句话来。不知道他们的太太会不会有趣一些。”

“在英国也会如此么，斯凯勋爵？”

===Original===
He looked down at her with half-shut eyes, and drawled: “You know my countrywomen?”

“Hardly at all.”

“Then let us discuss some less serious subject.”

“Willingly. I have waited for you to explain to me why you have tonight an expression of such melancholy.”

“Is that quite friendly, Mrs. Lee? Do I really look melancholy?”

“Unutterably, as I feel. I am consumed with curiosity to know the reason.”
===Chinese===
他半阖着眼俯视她，拖长了调子说：“您认识我们英国的女人？”

“几乎说不上认识。”

“那我们还是换个不那么严肃的话题吧。”

“乐意之至。我正等着您向我解释，今晚您何以面上带着这样一副忧郁的神情。”

“这么说够朋友么，李夫人？我瞧着真有那么忧郁么？”

“忧郁得无以复加，我的感觉如此。我好奇心熬得慌，非知道缘故不可。”

===Original===
The British minister coolly took a complete survey of the whole room, ending with a prolonged stare at the President and his wife, who were still mechanically shaking hands; then he looked back into her face, and said never a word.

She insisted: “I must have this riddle answered. It suffocates me. I should not be sad at seeing these same people at work or at play, if they ever do play; or in a church or a lecture-room. Why do they weigh on me like a horrid phantom here?”

“I see no riddle, Mrs. Lee. You have answered your own question; they are neither at work nor at play.”

“Then please take me home at once. I shall have hysterics. The sight of those two suffering images at the door is too mournful to be borne. I am dizzy with looking at these stalking figures. I don’t believe they’re real. I wish the house would take fire. I want an earthquake. I wish someone would pinch the President, or pull his wife’s hair.”
===Chinese===
英国公使不动声色地把整个厅堂扫视了一遍，末了对着仍在机械握手的总统和总统夫人盯视良久；随后他回眼望进她的脸，一言不发。

她不依不饶：“这个谜我非要个答案不可。它闷得我透不过气。同样这些人，我看着他们劳作、游玩——倘若他们当真游玩的话——或是坐在教堂里、讲堂里，都不会难过。为什么偏偏在这里，他们却像狰狞的鬼影一般压在我心上？”

“我看不出什么谜，李夫人。您已经自己解答了自己的问题：他们既不在劳作，也不在游玩。”

“那就请您立刻送我回家。我要发歇斯底里了。门口那两尊受难的形象，凄惨得叫人不忍再看。望着这些昂首阔步的人形，我头都晕了。我不信他们是真人。我恨不得这房子失火。我想来一场地震。我盼着有谁去拧总统一把，或是扯一扯他太太的头发。”

===Original===
Mrs. Lee did not repeat the experiment of visiting the White House, and indeed for some time afterwards she spoke with little enthusiasm of the presidential office. To Senator Ratcliffe she expressed her opinions strongly. The Senator tried in vain to argue that the people had a right to call upon their chief magistrate, and that he was bound to receive them; this being so, there was no less objectionable way of proceeding than the one which had been chosen. “Who gave the people any such right?” asked Mrs. Lee. “Where does it come from? What do they want it for? You know better, Mr. Ratcliffe! Our chief magistrate is a citizen like anyone else. What puts it into his foolish head to cease being a citizen and to ape royalty? Our governors never make themselves ridiculous. Why cannot the wretched being content himself with living like the rest of us, and minding his own business? Does he know what a figure of fun he is?” And Mrs. Lee went so far as to declare that she would like to be the President’s wife only to put an end to this folly; nothing should ever induce *her* to go through such a performance; and if the public did not approve of this, Congress might impeach her, and remove her from office; all she demanded was the right to be heard before the Senate in her own defence.
===Chinese===
李夫人没有再试第二回参观白宫；此后好一阵子，她提起总统这个职位来，也确实没了多少热忱。她对拉特克利夫参议员激烈地发表了看法。参议员徒劳地争辩说，人民有权谒见他们的最高行政长官，而长官也有义务接见他们；事既如此，已择定的这套做法已是可议之处最少的了。“谁给了人民这样的权利？”李夫人问。“这权利从哪里来？他们要它做什么？您是明白人，拉特克利夫先生！我们的最高行政长官不过是个同旁人一样的公民。是什么钻进了他那一颗蠢脑袋，叫他不再安于公民的身份，去仿效起王室来？我们的历任州长从不闹这种笑话。这个可怜虫为什么就不能安下心来，同我们其余人一样过日子、只管自己的事？他可知道自己成了个多大的笑柄？”李夫人甚至放言：她但愿做总统夫人，只为好把这桩蠢事革除净尽；无论如何也不能诱使她本人去行这么一套过场；公众若不答应，尽可以让国会弹劾她、将她免职；她所要求的全部，只是获准在参议院面前亲口自辩的权利。

===Original===
Nevertheless, there was a very general impression in Washington that Mrs. Lee would like nothing better than to be in the White House. Known to comparatively few people, and rarely discussing even with them the subjects which deeply interested her, Madeleine passed for a clever, intriguing woman who had her own objects to gain. True it is, beyond peradventure, that all residents of Washington may be assumed to be in office or candidates for office; unless they avow their object, they are guilty of an attempt﻿—and a stupid one﻿—to deceive; yet there is a small class of apparent exceptions destined at last to fall within the rule. Mrs. Lee was properly assumed to be a candidate for office. To the Washingtonians it was a matter of course that Mrs. Lee should marry Silas P. Ratcliffe. That he should be glad to get a fashionable and intelligent wife, with twenty or thirty thousand dollars a year, was not surprising. That she should accept the first public man of the day, with a flattering chance for the Presidency﻿—a man still comparatively young and not without good looks﻿—was perfectly natural, and in her undertaking she had the sympathy of all well-regulated Washington women who were not possible rivals; for to them the President’s wife is of more consequence than the President; and, indeed, if America only knew it, they are not very far from the truth.
===Chinese===
尽管如此，华盛顿却有一个十分普遍的看法：李夫人再没有什么比住进白宫更想要的了。马德琳相识者寥寥，即便对这几个人，她也极少谈及自己深切关注的题目，于是被人当作一个聪明而工于心计、自有图谋的女人。诚然，毋庸置疑：华盛顿的一切居民，皆可假定是在任官员或职位候选人；倘若不自陈来意，便犯了一桩欺瞒之罪——而且愚不可及——然而也有小小的一类表面例外，终究注定要归入这条通则。李夫人便被顺理成章地假定为一个候缺者。在华盛顿人看来，李夫人嫁给赛拉斯·P. 拉特克利夫，乃是理所当然之事。他乐得娶一位时髦而聪慧、岁入两三万美元的太太，不足为奇；她肯接纳当今第一号公职人物——且晋身总统的机会大为可观——此人尚算年轻，也并非全无相貌——更是再自然不过；她这番事业，还得了华盛顿所有规矩持重、又不致成为潜在情敌的太太们的一致同情；因为在她们看来，总统夫人比总统本人更要紧；而且，说实在的，美国若有自知之明，便知她们离真理不远。

===Original===
Some there were, however, who did not assent to this good-natured though worldly view of the proposed match. These ladies were severe in their comments upon Mrs. Lee’s conduct, and did not hesitate to declare their opinion that she was the calmest and most ambitious minx who had ever come within their observation. Unfortunately it happened that the respectable and proper Mrs. Schuyler Clinton took this view of the case, and made little attempt to conceal her opinion. She was justly indignant at her cousin’s gross worldliness, and possible promotion in rank.

“If Madeleine Ross marries that coarse, horrid old Illinois politician,” said she to her husband, “I never will forgive her so long as I live.”

Mr. Clinton tried to excuse Madeleine, and even went so far as to suggest that the difference of age was no greater than in their own case; but his wife trampled ruthlessly on his argument.

“At any rate,” said she, “I never came to Washington as a widow on purpose to set my cap for the first candidate for the Presidency, and I never made a public spectacle of my indecent eagerness in the very galleries of the Senate; and Mrs. Lee ought to be ashamed of herself. She is a cold-blooded, heartless, unfeminine cat.”
===Chinese===
然而也有人不肯赞同这桩拟议中的亲事那与人为善而毕竟势利的看法。这几位太太对李夫人的行止抨击得甚是严厉，并且毫不迟疑地公然宣布高见：在她们观察所及的范围里，李夫人是从来最沉得住气、野心也最大的一个小狐狸精。不巧的是，体面而端方的斯凯勒·克林顿夫人恰好持此看法，而且几乎无意掩饰自己的见解。她为堂妹那十足的势利、以及可能随之而来的位分擢升而义愤填膺，这义愤也自有其道理。

“马德琳·罗斯要是嫁给那个粗俗、可恶的伊利诺伊老政客，”她对丈夫说，“我到死也不会饶她。”

克林顿先生竭力替马德琳开脱，甚至进言说两人的年龄之差，并不比他们夫妇俩的更大；可他的太太无情地把他的论点踩了个稀烂。

“无论如何，”她说，“我当初可没有以寡妇之身专程跑来华盛顿，好向头一位总统候选人抛彩球；我也从没有在参议院的旁听席上，把自己那份不成体统的急切公然示众；李夫人应该为自己害臊。她是一只冷血、无心肝、没个女人样的猫。”

===Original===
Little Victoria Dare, who babbled like the winds and streams, with utter indifference as to what she said or whom she addressed, used to bring choice bits of this gossip to Mrs. Lee. She always affected a little stammer when she said anything uncommonly impudent, and put on a manner of languid simplicity. She felt keenly the satisfaction of seeing Madeleine charged with her own besetting sins. For years all Washington had agreed that Victoria was little better than one of the wicked; she had done nothing but violate every rule of propriety and scandalise every well-regulated family in the city, and there was no good in her. Yet it could not be denied that Victoria was amusing, and had a sort of irregular fascination; consequently she was universally tolerated. To see Mrs. Lee thrust down to her own level was an unmixed pleasure to her, and she carefully repeated to Madeleine the choice bits of dialogue which she picked up in her wanderings.

“Your cousin, Mrs. Clinton, says you are a ca‑ca‑cat, Mrs. Lee.”

“I don’t believe it, Victoria. Mrs. Clinton never said anything of the sort.”

“Mrs. Marston says it is because you have caught a ra‑ra‑rat, and Senator Clinton was only a m‑m‑mouse!”
===Chinese===
小维多利亚·戴尔（Victoria Dare）叽叽喳喳，像风又像溪，全不管自己说了什么、对谁说，常把这类闲话的精华送到李夫人耳朵里。每说一句格外无礼的话，她总要装出一点口吃，再摆出一副慵懒天真的姿态。眼见马德琳被按上她自己那些屡犯不悛的罪过，她感到一阵尖锐的快意。多年以来，华盛顿举城公认维多利亚比恶徒强不到哪里去；她一贯所为，无非践踏一切礼法、惊扰城中每一户规矩人家，一身一无是处。然而不可否认，维多利亚有趣，自有一种不合常规的魅惑；于是人人容得下她。眼看李夫人被拽落到自己的水平线上，于她是一桩纯粹的乐事；她便把游荡间拾来的对话精华，一字不苟地复述给马德琳。

“您那位堂姐克林顿夫人说，您是一只猫——猫——猫儿，李夫人。”

“我才不信呢，维多利亚。克林顿夫人从没说过这种话。”

“马斯顿夫人（Mrs. Marston）说，那是因为您逮住了一只大——大——大鼠，而克林顿参议员不过是一只小——小——小鼠！”

===Original===
Naturally all this unexpected publicity irritated Mrs. Lee not a little, especially when short and vague paragraphs, soon followed by longer and more positive ones, in regard to Senator Ratcliffe’s matrimonial prospects, began to appear in newspapers, along with descriptions of herself from the pens of enterprising female correspondents for the press, who had never so much as seen her. At the first sight of one of these newspaper articles, Madeleine fairly cried with mortification and anger. She wanted to leave Washington the next day, and she hated the very thought of Ratcliffe. There was something in the newspaper style so inscrutably vulgar, something so inexplicably revolting to the sense of feminine decency, that she shrank under it as though it were a poisonous spider. But after the first acute shame had passed, her temper was roused, and she vowed that she would pursue her own path just as she had begun, without regard to all the malignity and vulgarity in the wide United States. She did not care to marry Senator Ratcliffe; she liked his society and was flattered by his confidence; she rather hoped to prevent him from ever making a formal offer, and if not, she would at least push it off to the last possible moment; but she was not to be frightened from marrying him by any amount of spitefulness or gossip, and she did not mean to refuse him except for stronger reasons than these. She even went so far in her desperate courage as to laugh at her cousin, Mrs. Clinton, whose venerable husband she allowed and even encouraged to pay her such public attention and to express sentiments of such youthful ardour as she well knew would inflame and exasperate the excellent lady his wife.
===Chinese===
这一番突如其来的曝光，自然叫李夫人恼火不小，尤其是当报上开始出现一段段关于拉特克利夫参议员婚姻前景的短小含混的简讯——不久又跟上更长、更言之凿凿的篇什——连同那些野心勃勃的报馆女访员笔下对她的种种描摹，而这些人连她的面都不曾见过。头一眼看见这样一篇报章文字，马德琳又羞又恼，当真哭了。她恨不得第二天就离开华盛顿，而且一想到拉特克利夫便生恨。报章的笔调里有一种无从索解的俗气，有一种莫名刺犯女性体面之感的东西，她在这笔调底下瑟缩，仿佛那是一只毒蜘蛛。可是最初的尖锐羞耻过去之后，她的性子被激了起来，她发誓要照刚开头那样走自己的路，全然不理会偌大美国的一切恶意与粗俗。她并不想嫁给拉特克利夫参议员；她喜欢他的交往，也受用他的信任；她宁可设法叫他永远不开口正式求婚，退一步，也要把这事推到推无可推的最后一刻；但任凭多少怨毒与闲话，都休想吓住她不嫁给他；而要她拒绝他，也得有比这更硬的理由。她甚至凭着那股破釜沉舟的胆气，拿堂姐克林顿夫人打起趣来：克林顿夫人那位年高德劭的丈夫，她容许、甚至怂恿他对自己当众大献殷勤，任他倾诉那般少年人的热忱——她心里雪亮，这准会撩得他那位贤德夫人火冒三丈。

===Original===
Carrington was the person most unpleasantly affected by the course which this affair had taken. He could no longer conceal from himself the fact that he was as much in love as a dignified Virginian could be. With him, at all events, she had shown no coquetry, nor had she ever either flattered or encouraged him. But Carrington, in his solitary struggle against fate, had found her a warm friend; always ready to assist where assistance was needed, generous with her money in any cause which he was willing to vouch for, full of sympathy where sympathy was more than money, and full of resource and suggestion where money and sympathy failed. Carrington knew her better than she knew herself. He selected her books; he brought the last speech or the last report from the Capitol or the departments; he knew her doubts and her vagaries, and as far as he understood them at all, helped her to solve them. Carrington was too modest, and perhaps too shy, to act the part of a declared lover, and he was too proud to let it be thought that he wanted to exchange his poverty for her wealth. But he was all the more anxious when he saw the evident attraction which Ratcliffe’s strong will and unscrupulous energy exercised over her. He saw that Ratcliffe was steadily pushing his advances; that he flattered all Mrs. Lee’s weaknesses by the confidence and deference with which he treated her; and that in a very short time, Madeleine must either marry him or find herself looked upon as a heartless coquette. He had his own reasons for thinking ill of Senator Ratcliffe, and he meant to prevent a marriage; but he had an enemy to deal with not easily driven from the path, and quite capable of routing any number of rivals.
===Chinese===
这桩事态的走向，最感如芒刺在背的要数卡林顿。他再也无法向自己隐瞒：他恋爱了，爱得像一个庄重的弗吉尼亚人所可能有的那么深。至少对他，她从未卖弄过风情，也从未奉承过他、怂恿过他。但在卡林顿孤身对抗命运的挣扎里，他发现她是个热忱的朋友：哪里需要帮手，哪里总有她；凡他肯担保的事业，她花钱从不吝惜；在同情心比金钱更贵重的地方，她满腔同情；在金钱与同情都无济于事的地方，她满腹韬略与建议。卡林顿了解她，胜过她了解自己。他替她挑书；他从国会大厦或各部委带回最新的演说稿、最新的报告；他熟知她的疑虑和她的奇想，而且只要他还算理解，便帮她一一化解。卡林顿太自谦，也许还太腼腆，扮不成一个公开的求婚者；他又太高傲，不肯让人以为他想拿自己的清贫换她的财富。可当他眼见拉特克利夫那坚强的意志与不择手段的干劲对她施展出的显著引力，他愈发焦灼了。他看出拉特克利夫正在稳步推进；看出后者以信任与敬重待她，把她所有的弱点一一谀奉周到；看出用不了多久，马德琳要么嫁给他，要么落个没心肝的卖弄风情女子的名声。他对拉特克利夫参议员素有恶感，自有他的缘由，他也存心要阻成这桩婚事；只是他要对付的这个敌手不易驱离径路，而且完全有能力把任何数目的情敌杀个落花流水。

===Original===
Ratcliffe was afraid of no one. He had not fought his own way in life for nothing, and he knew all the value of a cold head and dogged self-assurance. Nothing but this robust Americanism and his strong will carried him safely through the snares and pitfalls of Mrs. Lee’s society, where rivals and enemies beset him on every hand. He was little better than a schoolboy, when he ventured on their ground, but when he could draw them over upon his own territory of practical life he rarely failed to trample on his assailants. It was this practical sense and cool will that won over Mrs. Lee, who was woman enough to assume that all the graces were well enough employed in decorating her, and it was enough if the other sex felt her superiority. Men were valuable only in proportion to their strength and their appreciation of women. If the senator had only been strong enough always to control his temper, he would have done very well, but his temper was under a great strain in these times, and his incessant effort to control it in politics made him less watchful in private life. Mrs. Lee’s tacit assumption of superior refinement irritated him, and sometimes made him show his teeth like a bulldog, at the cost of receiving from Mrs. Lee a quick stroke in return such as a well-bred tortoiseshell cat administers to check over-familiarity; innocent to the eye, but drawing blood. One evening when he was more than commonly out of sorts, after sitting some time in moody silence, he roused himself, and, taking up a book that lay on her table, he glanced at its title and turned over the leaves. It happened by ill luck to be a volume of Darwin that Mrs. Lee had just borrowed from the library of Congress.
===Chinese===
拉特克利夫谁也不怕。他这一生是一路厮杀出来的，绝非虚掷，他深知冷静的头脑与顽强的自信的全部价值。全凭这副茁壮的美国气性加上坚强的意志，他才安然穿过李夫人社交圈的重重圈套与陷阱——那里四面八方都是情敌与仇家。闯进他们的地界时，他比一个小学生强不了多少；可一旦把对手引到自己实干生活的地面上，他少有不把来犯者踩在脚下的。正是这份务实的头脑与冷静的意志收服了李夫人；她够一个女人，认定种种风雅才艺，用来装点自己已是得其所哉，至于异性，只消感受到她的优越，便已足够。男人的价值，只与其力量、与其对女人的赏识成正比。这位参议员倘若意志再坚强些、总能管住自己的性子，他会混得很好；可这些日子他的性子绷得极紧，政坛上无休止的克制，叫他在私生活里放松了警惕。李夫人那默认的高人一等的雅致激恼了他，有时叫他像斗牛犬似的龇出牙来，代价是挨李夫人疾快的一爪——像一只有教养的玳瑁猫为惩戒过分的亲昵而赏出的那一种：看去无伤，实则见血。一天晚上，他心绪比往常更坏，闷坐了半晌之后，提起精神，拿起她桌上一本书，瞥了瞥书名，翻了翻书页。倒霉的是，那恰是李夫人刚从国会图书馆借来的一部达尔文。

===Original===
“Do you understand this sort of thing?” asked the Senator abruptly, in a tone that suggested a sneer.

“Not very well,” replied Mrs. Lee, rather curtly.

“Why do you want to understand it?” persisted the Senator. “What good will it do you?”

“Perhaps it will teach us to be modest,” answered Madeleine, quite equal to the occasion.

“Because it says we descend from monkeys?” rejoined the Senator, roughly. “Do you think you are descended from monkeys?”

“Why not?” said Madeleine.

“Why not?” repeated Ratcliffe, laughing harshly. “I don’t like the connection. Do you mean to introduce your distant relations into society?”

“They would bring more amusement into it than most of its present members,” rejoined Mrs. Lee, with a gentle smile that threatened mischief.
===Chinese===
“这种东西您看得懂么？”参议员唐突地问，腔调里带着讥诮的意味。

“不很懂，”李夫人答道，相当简慢。

“那您何必想弄懂它呢？”参议员追问。“它对您有什么好处？”

“兴许它能教我们学会谦逊，”马德琳答道，应付这一场面绰绰有余。

“因为它说我们是猴子的后代？”参议员粗声反驳。“您以为自己也是猴子的后代？”

“为什么不是？”马德琳说。

“为什么不是？”拉特克利夫重复了一遍，笑声刺耳。“我不喜欢这门亲眷。您是打算把您那些远亲也引进社交界么？”

“他们能给它带来的乐趣，比它现有成员的大半都要多，”李夫人反唇答道，嫣然一笑，笑里藏着祸机。

===Original===
But Ratcliffe would not be warned; on the contrary, the only effect of Mrs. Lee’s defiance was to exasperate his ill-temper, and whenever he lost his temper he became senatorial and Websterian. “Such books,” he began, “disgrace our civilization; they degrade and stultify our divine nature; they are only suited for Asiatic despotisms where men are reduced to the level of brutes; that they should be accepted by a man like Baron Jacobi, I can understand; he and his masters have nothing to do in the world but to trample on human rights. Mr. Carrington, of course, would approve those ideas; he believes in the divine doctrine of flogging negroes; but that you, who profess philanthropy and free principles, should go with them, is astonishing; it is incredible; it is unworthy of you.”

“You are very hard on the monkeys,” replied Madeleine, rather sternly, when the Senator’s oration was ended. “The monkeys never did you any harm; they are not in public life; they are not even voters; if they were, you would be enthusiastic about their intelligence and virtue. After all, we ought to be grateful to them, for what would men do in this melancholy world if they had not inherited gaiety from the monkeys﻿—as well as oratory.”
===Chinese===
可拉特克利夫不肯受这个警告；恰恰相反，李夫人的挑衅唯一的效果，便是火上浇油，而且每逢乱了性子，他便摆出参议院的派头、韦伯斯特的腔调。“这样的书，”他开腔了，“玷污我们的文明；贬低并愚化我们神圣的天性；它们只配盛行于那种把人贬到畜生水平的亚洲专制国家。雅各比男爵那样的人接受它们，我能理解；他同他的主子们活在世上，除了践踏人权别无事做。卡林顿先生当然赞成那些主张；他信奉鞭打黑人的神圣教义；至于您——一个标榜博爱与自由原则的人——居然同他们一伙，实在惊人；难以置信；有失您的身份。”

“您对猴子也太苛刻了，”参议员的高论告一段落，马德琳相当严厉地答道。“猴子从没害过您；他们不在仕途；他们连选民都不是；若是，您准会对他们的智识与德行赞不绝口。说到底，我们还该感激他们呢，要不是从猴子那里继承了快活的天性——还有演说的天分——在这个愁苦的世界上，人还拿什么撑得住呢？”

===Original===
Ratcliffe, to do him justice, took punishment well, at least when it came from Mrs. Lee’s hands, and his occasional outbursts of insubordination were sure to be followed by improved discipline; but if he allowed Mrs. Lee to correct his faults, he had no notion of letting himself be instructed by her friends, and he lost no chance of telling them so. But to do this was not always enough. Whether it were that he had few ideas outside of his own experience, or that he would not trust himself on doubtful ground, he seemed compelled to bring every discussion down to his own level. Madeleine puzzled herself in vain to find out whether he did this because he knew no better, or because he meant to cover his own ignorance.
===Chinese===
平心而论，拉特克利夫受罚受得漂亮，至少出自李夫人之手的惩罚是如此；他偶尔发作的抗命，过后必是更见规矩的纪律。但李夫人可以矫正他的过失，他可万万不打算受她朋友们的调教，而且一有机会便向诸位言明。可单单这样还不够。不知是因为他本人的经验之外便没有多少主意，还是因为可疑的地面上他不肯托付自己，他仿佛总不得不把每一场讨论都拽到自己的水平线上。马德琳苦思而不得其解：他这样做，究竟是出于不懂更佳的道理，还是存心遮掩自己的无知。

===Original===
“The Baron has amused me very much with his account of Bucharest society,” Mrs. Lee would say: “I had no idea it was so gay.”

“I would like to show him our society in Peonia,” was Ratcliffe’s reply; “he would find a very brilliant circle there of nature’s true noblemen.”

“The Baron says their politicians are precious sharp chaps,” added Mr. French.

“Oh, there are politicians in Bulgaria, are there?” asked the Senator, whose ideas of the Romanian and Bulgarian neighbourhood were vague, and who had a general notion that all such people lived in tents, wore sheepskins with the wool inside, and ate curds: “Oh, they have politicians there! I would like to see them try their sharpness in the west.”

“Really!” said Mrs. Lee. “Think of Attila and his hordes running an Indiana caucus?”

“Anyhow,” cried French with a loud laugh, “the Baron said that a set of bigger political scoundrels than his friends couldn’t be found in all Illinois.”

“Did he say that?” exclaimed Ratcliffe angrily.

“Didn’t he, Mrs. Lee? but I don’t believe it; do you? What’s your candid opinion, Ratcliffe? What you don’t know about Illinois politics isn’t worth knowing; do you really think those Bulgrascals couldn’t run an Illinois state convention?”
===Chinese===
“男爵把布加勒斯特（Bucharest）社交界的情形讲给我听，讲得有趣极了，”李夫人常说：“我原先不知道那里这样热闹。”

“我倒乐意让他见识见识我们皮奥尼亚的社交界，”拉特克利夫答道；“他会发现那里有一圈极其显赫的、天生的真贵族。”

“男爵说，他们的政客可是顶顶精明的家伙，”弗伦奇先生加了一句。

“哦，保加利亚也有政客，是么？”参议员问；他对罗马尼亚、保加利亚一带的概念本就模糊，又笼统地以为那一带的人都住帐篷、穿毛朝里的羊皮袄、吃凝乳：“哦，那边居然也有政客！我倒想看看，他们把那份精明拿到西部来试试身手。”

“真的么！”李夫人说。“想想阿提拉（Attila）带着他的蛮族部众，来操办一场印第安纳（Indiana）的党团会议，是何光景！”

“不管怎么说，”弗伦奇放声大笑道，“男爵说了，比他那些朋友更厉害的政治恶棍，整个伊利诺伊也再找不出一伙。”

“他这么说了？”拉特克利夫怒喝。

“他不是说了么，李夫人？反正我不信；您信么？拉特克利夫，您有何坦率高见？伊利诺伊的政治，凡是您不知道的，便是不值得知道的；您当真以为那帮保加利亚恶棍（Bulgrascals，原文系“保加利亚人”与“恶棍”的合词双关）操办不了伊利诺伊州的党代表大会？”

===Original===
Ratcliffe did not like to be chaffed, especially on this subject, but he could not resent French’s liberty which was only a moderate return for the wooden nutmeg. To get the conversation away from Europe, from literature, from art, was his great object, and chaff was a way of escape.

Carrington was very well aware that the weak side of the Senator lay in his blind ignorance of morals. He flattered himself that Mrs. Lee must see this and be shocked by it sooner or later, so that nothing more was necessary than to let Ratcliffe expose himself. Without talking very much, Carrington always aimed at drawing him out. He soon found, however, that Ratcliffe understood such tactics perfectly, and instead of injuring, he rather improved his position. At times the man’s audacity was startling, and even when Carrington thought him hopelessly entangled, he would sweep away all the hunter’s nets with a sheer effort of strength, and walk off bolder and more dangerous than ever.
===Chinese===
拉特克利夫不喜欢被人打趣，尤其是在这个题目上，可他无法记恨弗伦奇的放肆——那不过是木头肉豆蔻一桩公道的小小回敬。叫谈话躲开欧洲、躲开文学、躲开艺术，是他的头等要务，而插科打诨正是一条脱身之路。

卡林顿心里十分清楚，这位参议员的软肋在于他对道德的全然无知。他暗自笃定，李夫人迟早必会看破这一点，并为之震惊，所以只需任拉特克利夫自行败露，再不必多费手脚。卡林顿言语不多，却总是存心引他开口。然而他很快发现，拉特克利夫对这类战术洞若观火，非但毫发无损，阵地反倒愈发稳固。此人的胆大妄为有时令人咋舌；即便卡林顿以为他已缠入罗网、万难脱身，他也会凭一股蛮力把猎人的网一扫而空，扬长而去，比先前更狂傲、也更危险。

===Original===
When Mrs. Lee pressed him too closely, he frankly admitted her charges. “What you say is in great part true. There is much in politics that disgusts and disheartens; much that is coarse and bad. I grant you there is dishonesty and corruption. We must try to make the amount as small as possible.”

“You should be able to tell Mrs. Lee how she must go to work,” said Carrington; “you have had experience. I have heard, it seems to me, that you were once driven to very hard measures against corruption.”

Ratcliffe looked ill-pleased at this compliment, and gave Carrington one of his cold glances that meant mischief. But he took up the challenge on the spot:﻿—
===Chinese===
李夫人逼得太紧的时候，他便坦率认账。“您说的大半是实情。政治里确有许多令人作呕、令人灰心的东西；许多粗鄙丑恶的东西。我向您承认，其中有欺诈，有贪腐。我们只能设法把这数额压到尽可能小。”

“您该能告诉李夫人，该当如何着手才是，”卡林顿说；“您是有过经验的。我记得听说，您有一次被迫对贪腐采取过非常强硬的手段。”

拉特克利夫听了这番恭维，面有愠色，赏了卡林顿一个含着杀机的冷眼。但他当场应下了这纸战书：

===Original===
“Yes, I was, and am very sorry for it. The story is this, Mrs. Lee; and it is well-known to every man, woman, and child in the State of Illinois, so that I have no reason for softening it. In the worst days of the war there was almost a certainty that my State would be carried by the peace party, by fraud, as we thought, although, fraud or not, we were bound to save it. Had Illinois been lost then, we should certainly have lost the Presidential election, and with it probably the Union. At any rate, I believed the fate of the war to depend on the result. I was then Governor, and upon me the responsibility rested. We had entire control of the northern counties and of their returns. We ordered the returning officers in a certain number of counties to make no returns until they heard from us, and when we had received the votes of all the southern counties and learned the precise number of votes we needed to give us a majority, we telegraphed to our northern returning officers to make the vote of their districts such and such, thereby overbalancing the adverse returns and giving the State to us. This was done, and as I am now senator I have a right to suppose that what I did was approved. I am not proud of the transaction, but I would do it again, and worse than that, if I thought it would save this country from disunion. But of course I did not expect Mr. Carrington to approve it. I believe he was then carrying out his reform principles by bearing arms against the government.”

“Yes!” said Carrington drily; “you got the better of me, too. Like the old Scotchman, you didn’t care who made the people’s wars provided you made its ballots.”

Carrington had missed his point. The man who has committed a murder for his country, is a patriot and not an assassin, even when he receives a seat in the Senate as his share of the plunder. Women cannot be expected to go behind the motives of that patriot who saves his country and his election in times of revolution.
===Chinese===
“是的，我被迫用过，而且深以为憾。事情是这样的，李夫人；这在伊利诺伊州男女老幼人人皆知，我没有理由把它说软。在战争最险恶的日子里，我的州几乎笃定要被主和派拿下——我们认定是靠舞弊，不过舞弊也罢、不舞弊也罢，我们非把它保住不可。伊利诺伊那时若是丢了，我们必定输掉总统选举，连带着多半还有合众国。至少我当时相信，战争的命运系于此役。我那时是州长，责任落在我一人身上。北方各县的选票申报，全在我们掌中。我们下令若干县申报选票的官员，未得我们通知一律不得申报；等南方各县的选票尽数到手、算清我们需要多少张票方能过半，我们便电令北方的申报官，把他们各区的票额造成某个数目，以此压倒不利的申报结果，把本州收归我们。事就这样办成了；如今我既已是参议员，我有权推想，我当年的所作所为是得到了嘉许的。这桩交易我并不引以为荣，但只要我认为它能救这个国家免于分裂，我不但会再来一次，还会干得更狠。当然啦，我并没指望卡林顿先生赞成。我记得他那时候，正靠着向政府开战来贯彻他的改革原则呢。”

“可不是！”卡林顿冷冷地说；“连我也栽在了您手里。您跟那个老苏格兰人一样：只要人民的选票由您来造，人民的仗由谁去打，您才不在乎呢。”

卡林顿失了算。为自己的国家行凶杀人者，是爱国志士，不是刺客——哪怕他在参议院里领一个席位，作为他那份赃物。至于那位在革命年代救了国家、也救了自己选举的爱国志士，他的动机深处，原不能指望女人们去追问。

===Original===
Carrington’s hostility to Ratcliffe was, however, mild, when compared with that felt by old Baron Jacobi. Why the baron should have taken so violent a prejudice it is not easy to explain, but a diplomatist and a senator are natural enemies, and Jacobi, as an avowed admirer of Mrs. Lee, found Ratcliffe in his way. This prejudiced and immoral old diplomatist despised and loathed an American senator as the type which, to his bleared European eyes, combined the utmost pragmatical self-assurance and overbearing temper with the narrowest education and the meanest personal experience that ever existed in any considerable government. As Baron Jacobi’s country had no special relations with that of the United States, and its Legation at Washington was a mere job to create a place for Jacobi to fill, he had no occasion to disguise his personal antipathies, and he considered himself in some degree as having a mission to express that diplomatic contempt for the Senate which his colleagues, if they felt it, were obliged to conceal. He performed his duties with conscientious precision. He never missed an opportunity to thrust the sharp point of his dialectic rapier through the joints of the clumsy and hidebound senatorial self-esteem. He delighted in skilfully exposing to Madeleine’s eyes some new side of Ratcliffe’s ignorance. His conversation at such times sparkled with historical allusions, quotations in half a dozen different languages, references to well-known facts which an old man’s memory could not recall with precision in all their details, but with which the Honourable Senator was familiarly acquainted, and which he could readily supply. And his Voltairian face leered politely as he listened to Ratcliffe’s reply, which showed invariable ignorance of common literature, art, and history. The climax of his triumph came one evening when Ratcliffe unluckily, tempted by some allusion to Molière which he thought he understood, made reference to the unfortunate influence of that great man on the religious opinions of his time. Jacobi, by a flash of inspiration, divined that he had confused Molière with Voltaire, and assuming a manner of extreme suavity, he put his victim on the rack, and tortured him with affected explanations and interrogations, until Madeleine was in a manner forced to interrupt and end the scene. But even when the senator was not to be lured into a trap, he could not escape assault. The baron in such a case would cross the lines and attack him on his own ground, as on one occasion, when Ratcliffe was defending his doctrine of party allegiance, Jacobi silenced him by sneering somewhat thus: “Your principle is quite correct, Mr. Senator. I, too, like yourself, was once a good party man: my party was that of the Church; I was ultramontane. Your party system is one of your thefts from our Church; your National Convention is our Ecumenic Council; you abdicate reason, as we do, before its decisions; and you yourself, Mr. Ratcliffe, you are a Cardinal. They are able men, those cardinals; I have known many; they were our best friends, but they were not reformers. Are you a reformer, Mr. Senator?”
===Chinese===
然而，比起老男爵雅各比对拉特克利夫的敌意来，卡林顿那份算是温和的了。男爵何以抱有这般强烈的偏见，不易解释；只是外交官与参议员本是天敌，而雅各比既公开倾慕李夫人，便觉拉特克利夫碍了他的道。这个心存偏见、不讲道德的外交老朽，鄙夷且憎恶美国参议员这一典型：在他那双昏花的欧洲眼睛看来，这一典型把登峰造极的自以为是与专横脾性，同任何像样的政府里都前所未见的褊狭教育与最鄙陋的个人阅历，集于一身。雅各比男爵的国家同美国并无特殊邦交，其驻华盛顿的公使馆，也本就是为给雅各比安插一个位置而虚设的一桩闲差，因此他无需掩饰个人的好恶，而且在某种程度上，他自认身负一桩使命：要代外交界说出那份对参议院的轻蔑——这份轻蔑，他的同僚们纵然心怀，也不得不掩藏。他履行起职责来兢兢业业、分毫不爽。他从不放过一个机会，把辩证法细剑的锋尖，从参议员那笨重而僵硬的自尊甲胄缝隙里搠进去。他洋洋得意地巧施手腕，让马德琳亲眼看见拉特克利夫无知的又一个新侧面。逢这种场合，他的谈吐便焕然生辉：满眼历史典故，五六种文字的引语，还有对种种尽人皆知的史实的征引——那些史实，老人的记忆已难把细节一一精确召回，而这位尊贵的参议员却熟稔于胸，随时可以补足。听着拉特克利夫的回答——那回答对寻常文学、艺术与历史的无知从不落空——他那张伏尔泰式的脸便彬彬有礼地斜睨起来。他的凯旋在某个晚上攀上了顶点：那晚拉特克利夫不幸被一处莫里哀（Molière）的引喻诱动——他自以为懂得——大发议论，谈起那位伟人生前对当世宗教见解的不幸影响。雅各比灵光一闪，看破他把莫里哀同伏尔泰（Voltaire）搅混了，便摆出极其温文的神态，把猎物送上刑架，用装腔作势的解释与讯问百般拷打，直到马德琳迫于情势不得不出面打断，收了这场戏。可即便参议员不肯入彀，也逃不过攻击。那种时候，男爵便会越出阵地，攻到他自家的地面上，比如有一回，拉特克利夫正在捍卫他的效党原则，雅各比如此这般讥诮得他哑口无言：“您的原则完全正确，参议员先生。同您一样，我从前也是个忠实的党员：我的党是教会那一党；我是越山派（ultramontane）。你们的政党制度，是从我们教会偷去的赃物之一；你们的全国代表大会，便是我们的大公会议；同我们一样，你们在它的决议面前放弃理性；而您本人，拉特克利夫先生，您就是一位枢机主教。枢机主教们都是干才，我认识许多位；他们是我们最好的朋友，但他们不是改革派。您是改革派么，参议员先生？”

===Original===
Ratcliffe grew to dread and hate the old man, but all his ordinary tactics were powerless against this impenetrable eighteenth century cynic. If he resorted to his Congressional practise of browbeating and dogmatism, the Baron only smiled and turned his back, or made some remark in French which galled his enemy all the more, because, while he did not understand it, he knew well that Madeleine did, and that she tried to repress her smile. Ratcliffe’s grey eyes grew colder and stonier than ever as he gradually perceived that Baron Jacobi was carrying on a set scheme with malignant ingenuity, to drive him out of Madeleine’s house, and he swore a terrible oath that he would not be beaten by that monkey-faced foreigner. On the other hand Jacobi had little hope of success: “What can an old man do?” said he with perfect sincerity to Carrington; “If I were forty years younger, that great oaf should not have his own way. Ah! I wish I were young again and we were in Vienna!” From which it was rightly inferred by Carrington that the venerable diplomatist would, if such acts were still in fashion, have coolly insulted the Senator, and put a bullet through his heart.
===Chinese===
拉特克利夫渐渐既怕且恨这老头，可他惯用的战术，在这个刀枪不入的十八世纪犬儒面前一概无力。他若使出国会里那套恐吓与武断的惯技，男爵只微微一笑，转过背去，要么甩下一句法语——这句法语格外磨得敌人牙痒，因为他虽然听不懂，却深知马德琳听得懂，还知道她在强忍笑意。随着他渐渐看破，雅各比男爵正以恶毒的机巧推行着一整套定计，要把他从马德琳的宅子里驱赶出去，拉特克利夫那双灰眼睛变得比以往更冷、更硬如顽石，他发了狠誓：绝不能败给那个猴脸的外国佬。另一方面，雅各比自己也成功无望：“一个老头子还能做什么？”他一脸至诚地对卡林顿说；“我要是年轻四十岁，那个大蠢汉休想随心所欲。啊！我真想再年轻一回，我们俩又回到维也纳去！”卡林顿由此推断得不差：这位年高德劭的外交家，倘若此类行径犹在时兴，定会不动声色地羞辱参议员一场，再送一颗子弹，穿过他的心脏。

## VI / 六

===Original===
In February the weather became warmer and summer-like. In Virginia there comes often at this season a deceptive gleam of summer, slipping in between heavy storm-clouds of sleet and snow; days and sometimes weeks when the temperature is like June; when the earliest plants begin to show their hardy flowers, and when the bare branches of the forest trees alone protest against the conduct of the seasons. Then men and women are languid; life seems, as in Italy, sensuous and glowing with colour; one is conscious of walking in an atmosphere that is warm, palpable, radiant with possibilities; a delicate haze hangs over Arlington, and softens even the harsh white glare of the Capitol; the struggle of existence seems to abate; Lent throws its calm shadow over society; and youthful diplomatists, unconscious of their danger, are lured into asking foolish girls to marry them; the blood thaws in the heart and flows out into the veins, like the rills of sparkling water that trickle from every lump of ice or snow, as though all the ice and snow on earth, and all the hardness of heart, all the heresy and schism, all the works of the devil, had yielded to the force of love and to the fresh warmth of innocent, lamblike, confiding virtue. In such a world there should be no guile﻿—but there is a great deal of it notwithstanding. Indeed, at no other season is there so much. This is the moment when the two whited sepulchres at either end of the Avenue reek with the thick atmosphere of bargain and sale. The old is going; the new is coming. Wealth, office, power are at auction. Who bids highest? who hates with most venom? who intrigues with most skill? who has done the dirtiest, the meanest, the darkest, and the most, political work? He shall have his reward.

===Chinese===
