Do you know, Mr. Carrington, I’m not clever or serious, like Madeleine, and I can’t read laws, and hate politics, but I’ve more common sense than she has, and she makes me cross with her. I understand now why young widows are dangerous, and why they’re burned at their husband’s funerals in India. Not that I want to have Madeleine burned, for she’s a dear, good creature, and I love her better than anything in the world; but she will certainly do herself some dreadful mischief one of these days; she has the most extravagant notions about self-sacrifice and duty; if she hadn’t luckily thought of taking charge of me, she would have done some awful thing long ago, and if I could only be a little wicked, she would be quite happy all the rest of her life in reforming me; but now she has got hold of that Mr. Ratcliffe, and he is trying to make her think she can reform him, and if he does, it’s all up with us. Madeleine will just go and break her heart over that odious, great, coarse brute, who only wants her money.”

Sybil delivered this little oration with a degree of energy that went to Carrington’s heart. She did not often make such sustained efforts, and it was clear that on this subject she had exhausted her whole mind. Carrington was delighted, and urged her on. “I dislike Mr. Ratcliffe as much as you do;﻿—more perhaps. So does everyone who knows much about him. But we shall only make the matter worse if we interfere. What can we do?”

===Chinese===
西比尔一口把这话回绝了个干净：“她没来我才高兴呢。她要是来了，你就得一路同她讲话，把我撂在一边自己找乐子。你们俩准要讨论起来，我顶讨厌讨论。她要四处搜寻她的根本原理，你就得东奔西跑，替她捉几条来。再说，哪个礼拜天她自己要来的，带着那个讨厌的拉特克利夫先生。我实在看不出那人身上有什么好给她解闷的。她的品味在华盛顿是学坏了。您知道吗，卡林顿先生，我不像马德琳那么聪明、那么严肃，我读不懂法律，讨厌政治，可我的常识比她多，她真叫我冒火。我这下算明白了，年轻寡妇为什么危险，印度人为什么在丈夫的葬礼上把她们烧掉。倒不是我要把马德琳烧掉，她是个顶可爱、顶好心的人，我爱她胜过世上一切；可她早晚有一天要给自己闯出大祸来；她那套自我牺牲啦、本分啦的念头，奢侈得没了边；幸亏她想起来照管我，不然她早干出什么了不得的事了；我只要能学坏那么一点点，她后半辈子忙着改造我，就该快活死了；可如今她攥住了那位拉特克利夫先生，他又一心要叫她相信，她能改造他，他若真成了，我们就全完了。马德琳这一去，准要把心碎在那个可恶、粗大、下作的畜生身上——他要的不过是她的钱。”

西比尔发表这通小小演说时使出的那股劲头，一直说到卡林顿心坎里。她难得这样一气呵成地使劲，显然，在这个题目上她已倾其所有。卡林顿乐坏了，只管撺掇她往下说。“我同您一样不喜欢拉特克利夫先生；——没准更甚。凡是深知他底细的人，没有一个喜欢得起来。可我们一插手，只会把事情弄得更糟。我们又能做什么呢？”

===Original===
“That is just what I tell everybody,” resumed Sybil. “There is Victoria Dare always telling me I ought to do something; and Mr. Schneidekoupon too; just as though I could do anything. Madeleine has done nothing but get into mischief here. Half the people think her worldly and ambitious. Only last night that spiteful old woman, Mrs. Clinton, said to me: ‘Your sister is quite spoiled by Washington. She is more wild for power than any human being I ever saw.’ I was dreadfully angry and told her she was quite mistaken﻿—Madeleine was not the least spoiled. But I couldn’t say that she was not fond of power, for she is; but not in the way Mrs. Clinton meant. You should have seen her the other evening when Mr. Ratcliffe said about some matter of public business that he would do whatever she thought right; she spoke up quite sharply for her, with a scornful little laugh, and said that he had better do what *he* thought right. He looked for a moment almost angry, and muttered something about women’s being incomprehensible. He is always trying to tempt her with power. She might have had long ago all the power he could give her, but I can see, and he sees too, that she always keeps him at arm’s length. He doesn’t like it, but he expects one of these days to find a bribe that will answer. I wish we had never come to Washington. New York is so much nicer and the people there are much more amusing; they dance ever so much better and send one flowers all the time, and then they never talk about first principles. Maude had her hospitals and paupers and training school, and got along very well. It was so safe. But when I say so to her, she only smiles in a patronising kind of way, and tells me that I shall have as much of Newport as I want; just as though I were a child, and not a woman of twenty-five. Poor Maude! I can’t stay with her if she marries Mr. Ratcliffe, and it would break my heart to leave her with that man. Do you think he would beat her? Does he drink? I would almost rather be beaten a little, if I cared for a man, than be taken out to Peonia. Oh, Mr. Carrington! you are our only hope. She will listen to you. Don’t let her marry that dreadful politician.”

===Chinese===
“我跟谁都是这么说的，”西比尔接着讲。“维多利亚·戴尔老是对我说，我该有所作为；施奈德库彭先生也是；说得倒好像我真成得了什么似的。马德琳在这儿除了惹是非，什么也没干成。一半的人说她俗气、有野心。就在昨晚，那个促狭的老太婆克林顿夫人还对我说：‘你妹妹全让华盛顿惯坏了。对权力的那股疯劲，我生平没见过第二个。’我气得要命，告诉她大错特错——马德琳一点也没有惯坏。可她喜欢权力这话，我说不出口，因为她确实喜欢；只是不是克林顿夫人说的那种喜欢。前几天晚上，拉特克利夫先生说起一桩公事，说他愿照她认为对的去办，那情形您真该看看；她当即十分尖利地顶了回去，带着一声轻蔑的浅笑，说他还是做*他*自己认为对的事为好。他有一瞬间几乎要动怒，咕哝了一句女人没法懂之类的话。他老是拿权力诱惑她。他给得起的那份权力，她早就可以全数到手了，可我看得出——他也看得出——她始终同他保持一臂之遥。他不痛快，可他指望早晚寻出一件能奏效的贿赂来。我但愿我们从没来过华盛顿。纽约好得多，那儿的人也有趣得多；舞跳得好不知多少，还老送花给你，而且从来不谈什么根本原理。莫德有她的医院、贫民和培训学校，过得挺好。多安稳。可我这么同她说，她只施施然一笑，屈尊纡贵地告诉我，纽波特我爱要多少就要多少；说得好像我是个孩子，不是个二十五岁的女人。可怜的莫德！她若嫁给拉特克利夫先生，我就没法再跟着她了；把她丢给那个男人，又真能把我的心撕碎。您说他会不会打她？他喝酒吗？我要是看上了哪个男人，宁可挨他几下打，也不愿被他带到皮奥尼亚去。哦，卡林顿先生！您是我们唯一的指望了。她肯听您的。千万别叫她嫁给那个可怕的政客。”

===Original===
To all this pathetic appeal, some parts of which were as little calculated to please Carrington as Ratcliffe himself, Carrington answered that he was ready to do all in his power but that Sybil must tell him when and how to act.

“Then, it’s a bargain,” said she; “whenever I want you, I shall call on you for help, and you shall prevent the marriage.”

“Alliance offensive and defensive,” said he, laughing; “war to the knife on Ratcliffe. We will have his scalp if necessary, but I rather think he will soon commit hari-kari himself if we leave him alone.”

===Chinese===
这番恳切的呼吁——其中有些部分，叫卡林顿听着不见得比拉特克利夫本人更受用——卡林顿的答复是：他愿意尽他所能，但西比尔必须告诉他，何时动手，怎样动手。

“那可就说定了，”她说；“往后我要用得着你，便来找你搭把手，这桩婚事，就交给你来阻止。”

“攻守同盟，”他笑道；“对拉特克利夫刀刀见血。必要的话，我们就剥他的头皮；不过我倒觉得，只要由他去，不久他自己就会剖腹的。”

===Original===
“Madeleine will like him all the better if he does anything Japanese,” replied Sybil, with great seriousness; “I wish there was more Japanese bric-a-brac here, or any kind of old pots and pans to talk about. A little art would be good for her. What a strange place this is, and how people do stand on their heads in it! Nobody thinks like anyone else. Victoria Dare says she is trying on principle not to be good, because she wants to keep some new excitements for the next world. I’m sure she practices as she preaches. Did you see her at Mrs. Clinton’s last night. She behaved more outrageously than ever. She sat on the stairs all through supper, looking like a demure yellow cat with two bouquets in her paws﻿—and I know Lord Dunbeg sent one of them;﻿—and she actually let Mr. French feed her with ice-cream from a spoon. She says she was showing Lord Dunbeg a phase, and that he is going to put it into his article on American Manners and Customs in the *Quarterly*, but I don’t think it’s nice, do you, Mr. Carrington? I wish Madeleine had her to take care of. She would have enough to do then, I can tell her.”

===Chinese===
“他要真干了什么日本派头的举动，马德琳只会更喜欢他，”西比尔一本正经地答道；“我倒盼着这儿多几件日本小古玩，或者随便什么可供谈论的旧瓶旧罐。沾一点艺术，对她有好处。这地方多怪呀，人人都在里头倒立着过日子！谁跟谁想的都不一样。维多利亚·戴尔说，她正在照原则学坏，好给来世留些新鲜的刺激。我担保她言行如一。昨晚克林顿夫人家，您见着她了吧。她闹得比哪一回都出格。一顿晚饭的工夫，她全坐在楼梯上，活像一只端端庄庄的黄猫，两只前爪各攥着一束花——我知道其中一束是邓贝格勋爵送的——她还当真让弗伦奇先生拿茶匙舀着冰淇淋喂她。她说她这是让邓贝格勋爵见识一个侧面，他要把这个写进《评论季刊》上那篇论美国风俗习惯的文章里去。可我瞧着不体面，您说呢，卡林顿先生？我真希望马德琳把她领去管教。到那时她可有得忙了，这话我是敢跟她说的。”

===Original===
And so, gently prattling, Miss Sybil returned to the city, her alliance with Carrington completed; and it was a singular fact that she never again called him dull. There was henceforward a look of more positive pleasure and cordiality on her face when he made his appearance wherever she might be; and the next time he suggested a horseback excursion she instantly agreed to go, although aware that she had promised a younger gentleman of the diplomatic body to be at home that same afternoon, and the good fellow swore polyglot oaths on being turned away from her door.

Mr. Ratcliffe knew nothing of this conspiracy against his peace and prospects. Even if he had known it, he might only have laughed, and pursued his own path without a second thought. Yet it was certain that he did not think Carrington’s enmity a thing to be overlooked, and from the moment of his obtaining a clue to its cause, he had begun to take precautions against it. Even in the middle of the contest for the Treasury, he had found time to listen to Mr. Wilson Keens report on the affairs of the late Samuel Baker. Mr. Keen came to him with a copy of Baker’s will and with memoranda of remarks made by the unsuspecting Mrs. Baker; “from which it appears,” said he, “that Baker, having no time to put his affairs in order, left special directions that his executors should carefully destroy all papers that might be likely to compromise individuals.”

===Chinese===
西比尔小姐就这样轻轻絮语着回到城里，她同卡林顿的同盟宣告成立；说来也怪，她从此再没说过他乏味。打这以后，不论在什么场合，只要他一露面，她脸上便添出一分更分明的欢喜与热络；下一回他再提议骑马出游，她一口应承，尽管心里明明白白记着，那天下午她已答应了外交团里一位更年轻的先生在家恭候，害得那位好性儿的年轻人吃了闭门羹，用好几国的语言赌咒发了个够。

这场针对他的安宁与前程的密谋，拉特克利夫先生一无所知。就算知道，他多半也不过付之一笑，照走自己的路，绝不多想。然而有一点是确凿的：他并不认为卡林顿的敌意是桩可以等闲视之的小事；而自打摸着其根由的线索那一刻起，他便着手加以提防了。甚至在与财政部长一职的争夺正酣之际，他还抽空听取了威尔逊·基恩先生关于已故的塞缪尔·贝克身后事宜的报告。基恩先生带来一份贝克遗嘱的抄件，还带着毫无戒心的贝克夫人所谈言语的备忘录；“由此可见，”他说，“贝克来不及把身后之事料理清楚，特意留下专门的指示，命遗嘱执行人悉数销毁一切可能牵连旁人的文件。”

===Original===
“What is the executor’s name?” interrupted Ratcliffe.

“The executor’s name is﻿—John Carrington,” said Keen, methodically referring to his copy of the will.

Ratcliffe’s face was impassive, but the inevitable, “I knew it,” almost sprang to his lips. He was rather pleased at the instinct which had led him so directly to the right trail.

Keen went on to say that from Mrs. Baker’s conversation it was certain that the testator’s directions had been carried out, and that the great bulk of these papers had been burned.

“Then it will be useless to press the inquiry further,” said Ratcliffe; “I am much obliged to you for your assistance,” and he turned the conversation to the condition of Mr. Keen’s bureau in the Treasury department.

===Chinese===
“遗嘱执行人叫什么名字？”拉特克利夫插话道。

“遗嘱执行人名叫——约翰·卡林顿，”基恩说着，一板一眼地翻着他那份遗嘱抄件。

拉特克利夫面无表情，可那句免不了的“我早知道”，几乎要蹦到嘴边。那份直觉径直把他引上了正路，他心中颇为得意。

基恩接着说，从贝克夫人的言谈可以断定，立遗嘱人的指示业已照办，这批文件绝大部分业已烧毁。

“那么，再追查下去也是无益了，”拉特克利夫说；“您的协助，多多拜领。”随即他把话头转到了基恩先生在财政部那个局的境况上去。

===Original===
The next time Ratcliffe saw Mrs. Lee, after his appointment to the Treasury was confirmed, he asked her whether she did not think Carrington very well suited for public service, and when she warmly assented, he said it had occurred to him to offer the place of Solicitor of the Treasury to Mr. Carrington, for although the actual salary might not be very much more than he earned by his private practice, the incidental advantages to a Washington lawyer were considerable; and to the Secretary it was especially necessary to have a solicitor in whom he could place entire confidence. Mrs. Lee was pleased by this motion of Ratcliffe’s, the more because she had supposed that Ratcliffe had no liking for Carrington. She doubted whether Carrington would accept the place, but she hoped that it might modify his dislike for Ratcliffe, and she agreed to sound him on the subject. There was something a little compromising in thus allowing herself to appear as the dispenser of Mr. Ratcliffe’s patronage, but she dismissed this objection on the ground that Carrington’s interests were involved, and that it was for him to judge whether he should take the place or not. Perhaps the world would not be so charitable if the appointment were made. What then? Mrs. Lee asked herself the question and did not feel quite at ease.

===Chinese===
财政部长的任命一经确认，拉特克利夫再见到李夫人时，便问她是否觉得卡林顿很适合担任公职；等她热切地表示赞同，他便说，他想到可以把财政部法务官的位子授给卡林顿先生——虽说实薪未必比他私人执业挣的多多少，可对一位华盛顿律师，种种附带的好处相当可观；而对部长来说，尤其少不了要有一位他能完全信得过的法务官。拉特克利夫这一举动叫李夫人心里受用，尤其因为她原以为拉特克利夫对卡林顿并无好感。她拿不准卡林顿肯不肯就任，但指望这或可冲淡他对拉特克利夫的恶感，便答应去探探他的口风。听任自己以拉特克利夫先生官缺分发者的姿态出面，这里面总有一点不大清白的地方；但她把这点异议打发了，理由是这事牵涉卡林顿的利益，就任与否，理应由他自己裁断。倘若任命当真成了，世人未必这样宽厚。那又怎样呢？李夫人问了自己这一句，心里总归不太踏实。

===Original===
So far as Carrington was concerned, she might have dismissed her doubts. There was not a chance of his taking the place, as very soon appeared. When she spoke to him on the subject, and repeated what Ratcliffe had said, his face flushed, and he sat for some moments in silence. He never thought very rapidly, but now the ideas seemed to come so fast as to bewilder his mind. The situation flashed before his eyes like electric sparks. His first impression was that Ratcliffe wanted to buy him; to tie his tongue; to make him run, like a fastened dog, under the wagon of the Secretary of the Treasury. His second notion was that Ratcliffe wanted to put Mrs. Lee under obligations, in order to win her regard; and, again, that he wanted to raise himself in her esteem by posing as a friend of honest administration and unassisted virtue. Then suddenly it occurred to him that the scheme was to make him appear jealous and vindictive; to put him in an attitude where any reason he might give for declining would bear a look of meanness, and tend to separate him from Mrs. Lee. Carrington was so absorbed by these thoughts, and his mind worked so slowly, that he failed to hear one or two remarks addressed to him by Mrs. Lee, who became a little alarmed, under the impression that he was unexpectedly paralyzed.

===Chinese===
单就卡林顿而论，她本可以把疑虑撇开。他绝无就任的可能，这一点很快就见了分晓。她向他提起这事、转述了拉特克利夫的话时，他的脸红了，坐着半晌不作声。他向来得慢，可这回念头来得又急又密，几乎把他的脑子搅昏。整个局面像电火花似的在他眼前乱闪。他的第一个印象是，拉特克利夫要收买他；要封住他的舌头；要叫他像条拴牢的狗，在财政部长的大车底下跟着跑。第二个念头是，拉特克利夫要让李夫人欠下人情，好赢取她的青睐；再一想，他又是要摆出一副清廉政治与自力美德之友的面孔，好在她眼里抬高自己。忽然他又想到，这套算计是要叫他显出一副妒火中烧、睚眦必报的嘴脸；把他架到一个位置上，使得他无论拿什么理由辞让，都透着一股小家子气，并且一步步把他同李夫人隔开。卡林顿全神贯注在这些念头上，脑子又转得慢，竟没听见李夫人对他讲的一两句话；李夫人有点着慌，只当他猝然瘫住了。

===Original===
When at length he heard her and attempted to frame an answer, his embarrassment increased. He could only stammer that he was sorry to be obliged to decline, but this office was one he could not undertake.

If Madeleine felt a little relieved by this decision, she did not show it. From her manner one might have supposed it to be her fondest wish that Carrington should be Solicitor of the Treasury. She cross-questioned him with obstinacy. Was not the offer a good one?﻿—and he was obliged to confess that it was. Were the duties such as he could not perform? Not at all! there was nothing in the duties which alarmed him. Did he object to it because of his southern prejudices against the administration? Oh, no! he had no political feeling to stand in his way. What, then, could be his reason for refusing?

===Chinese===
待到他终于听见她的话、想拼凑一个回答时，他的窘迫又添了几分。他只能结结巴巴地说，很抱歉不得不推辞，这个职位他担当不起。

这个决定若叫马德琳松了口气，她可没露在脸上。瞧她那副做派，人家还只当她朝思暮想的，正是卡林顿出任财政部法务官呢。她执拗地连连盘问。这份差事还不够好么？——他只得承认是好。那些职务他担当不了么？哪儿的话！职务里没有一样吓得住他。他推辞，是出于南方人对这届政府的成见么？哦，不！他没有任何政治情绪横在道中间。那么，他拒绝的理由究竟是什么呢？

===Original===
Carrington resorted again to silence, until Mrs. Lee, a little impatiently, asked whether it was possible that his personal dislike to Racliffe could blind him so far as to make him reject so fair a proposal. Carrington, finding himself more and more uncomfortable, rose restlessly from his chair and paced the room. He felt that Ratclife had fairly outgeneraled him, and he was at his wits’ end to know what card he could play that would not lead directly into Ratcliffe’s trump suit. To refuse such an offer was hard enough at best, for a man who wanted money and professional advancement as he did, but to injure himself and help Ratcliffe by this refusal, was abominably hard. Nevertheless, he was obliged to admit that he would rather not take a position so directly under Ratcliffe’s control. Madeleine said no more, but he thought she looked annoyed, and he felt himself in an intolerably painful situation. He was not certain that she herself might not have had some share in proposing the plan, and that his refusal might not have some mortifying consequences for her. What must she think of him, then? At this very moment he would have given his right arm for a word of real affection from Mrs. Lee. He adored her. He would willingly enough have damned himself for her. There was no sacrifice he would not have made to bring her nearer to him. In his upright, quiet, simple kind of way, he immolated himself before her. For months his heart had ached with this hopeless passion. He recognized that it was hopeless. He knew that she would never love him, and, to do her justice, she never had given him reason to suppose that it was in her power to love him, or any man. And here he stood, obliged to appear ungrateful and prejudiced, mean and vindictive, in her eyes. He took his seat again, looking so unutterably dejected, his patient face so tragically mournful, that Madeleine, after a while, began to see the absurd side of the matter, and presently burst into a laugh.

===Chinese===
卡林顿又祭起沉默来，直到李夫人带几分不耐烦地问：难道他对拉特克利夫个人的嫌恶，真能蒙蔽他到这个地步，教他把这么一份体面的提议推出去？卡林顿越发如坐针毡，烦躁地从椅子上起身，在屋里来回踱步。他只觉得拉特克利夫分明棋高一着，把他制得死死的；他绞尽脑汁，也想不出还能打出哪一张牌，不至于一头栽进拉特克利夫那套王牌里去。像他这样既缺钱、又巴望业务上更上层楼的人，拒绝这样一份差事，本来已够难堪；而这般一拒，既伤了自己、又成全了拉特克利夫，更是难堪到了极处。话虽如此，他也不得不承认，他实在不愿就任一个直接捏在拉特克利夫手心里的职位。马德琳不再言语，可他觉得她面有愠色，于是只觉得自己陷在一处难以忍受的苦境里。他吃不准这主意是否也有她的一份，也吃不准自己这一拒，会不会连累她受窘。那么，此刻她心里把他看成什么样的人呢？就在这一刻，只要能从李夫人口中听到一句真心爱怜的话，他情愿豁出一条右臂。他倾慕她。为了她，他万劫不复也甘愿。只要能叫她向自己靠近一步，没有什么牺牲是他不肯做的。他以他那种正直、安静、朴拙的方式，把自己当祭牲献在她面前。几个月来，他的心一直为这场无望的痴情隐隐作痛。他明白这是无望的。他知道她永远不会爱他；说句公道话，她也从没给过他理由，让他以为她有本事爱上他，或爱上任何男人。而此刻他站在她面前，在她的眼里，不得不显出一副忘恩负义、心怀偏见、小气记仇的样子。他重新落座，那副说不出的颓丧，那张坚忍的脸上的惨恻，看得马德琳过了一阵，竟看出这事可笑的一面来，扑哧一声笑了出来。

===Original===
“Please do not look so frightfully miserable!” said she; “I did not mean to make you unhappy. After all, what does it matter? You have a perfect right to refuse, and, for my part, I have not the least wish to see you accept.”

On this, Carrington brightened, and declared that if she thought him right in declining, he cared for nothing else. It was only the idea of hurting her feelings that weighed on his mind. But in saying this, he spoke in a tone that implied a deeper feeling, and made Mrs. Lee again look grave and sigh.

“Ah, Mr. Carrington,” she said, “this world will not run as we want. Do you suppose the time will ever come when everyone will be good and happy and do just what they ought? I thought this offer might possibly take one anxiety off your shoulders. I am sorry now that I let myself be led into making it.”

===Chinese===
“求你别摆出这副惨兮兮的样子了！”她说；“我不是有意叫你不痛快。说到底，这有什么要紧？你拒绝是完全有道理的；至于我，也一点不想看你应承下来。”

这话一出，卡林顿顿时眉目开朗，声言只要她觉得他辞得对，其余的他一概不放在心上。压在他心头的，只是怕伤了她感情这一层。可说这话时，他的语气里透着一股更深的情意，叫李夫人重新敛容，叹了一口气。

“唉，卡林顿先生，”她说，“这个世界不肯照我们的意思转。你想，那样的日子究竟会不会来——人人都善良、都快活、都各行其本分？我还道这份差事兴许能从你肩上卸下一桩心事。如今倒后悔，自己怎么就由着人牵了这个头。”

===Original===
Carrington could not answer her. He dared not trust his voice. He rose to go, and as she held out her hand, he suddenly raised it to his lips, and so left her. She sat for a moment with tears in her eyes after he was gone. She thought she knew all that was in his mind, and with a woman’s readiness to explain every act of men by their consuming passions for her own sex, she took it as a matter of course that jealousy was the whole cause of Carrington’s hostility to Ratcliffe, and she pardoned it with charming alacrity. “Ten years ago, I could have loved him,” she thought to herself, and then, while she was half smiling at the idea, suddenly another thought flashed upon her, and she threw her hand up before her face as though someone had struck her a blow. Carrington had reopened the old wound.

===Chinese===
卡林顿答不上来。他不敢信自己的声音。他起身告辞；她伸出手来，他忽然把它举到唇边吻了一下，就此别过。他走后，她噙着泪坐了片刻。她自以为洞悉他心里的一切，又怀着女人家那份现成，拿男人对自己同性的一腔痴念来解释他们的每一举动；于是她认定，卡林顿对拉特克利夫的全部敌意不过出于妒忌，并且以动人的爽利宽恕了它。“十年前，我本可以爱他的，”她暗自想道；随即，她正为这念头浮起半丝笑意，另一个念头电光般闪来，她猛地抬手掩在脸前，仿佛叫人打了一拳。卡林顿把那道旧伤重新挑开了。

===Original===
When Ratcliffe came to see her again, which he did very shortly afterwards, glad of so good an excuse, she told him of Carrington’s refusal, adding only that he seemed unwilling to accept any position that had a political character. Ratcliffe showed no sign of displeasure; he only said, in a benignant tone, that he was sorry to be unable to do something for so good a friend of hers; thus establishing, at all events, his claim on her gratitude. As for Carrington, the offer which Ratcliffe had made was not intended to be accepted, and Carrington could not have more embarrassed the secretary than by closing with it. Ratcliffe’s object had been to settle for his own satisfaction the question of Carrington’s hostility, for he knew the man well enough to feel sure that in any event he would act a perfectly straightforward part. If he accepted, he would at least be true to his chief. If he refused, as Ratcliffe expected, it would be a proof that some means must be found of getting him out of the way. In any case the offer was a new thread in the net that Mr. Ratcliffe flattered himself he was rapidly winding about the affections and ambitions of Mrs. Lee. Yet he had reasons of his own for thinking that Carrington, more easily than any other man, could cut the meshes of this net if he chose to do so, and therefore that it would be wiser to postpone action until Carrington were disposed of.

Without a moment’s delay he made inquiries as to all the vacant or eligible offices in the gift of the government outside his own department. Very few of these would answer his purpose. He wanted some temporary law business that would for a time take its holder away to a distance, say to Australia or Central Asia, the further the better; it must be highly paid, and it must be given in such a way as not to excite suspicion that Ratcliffe was concerned in the matter. Such an office was not easily found. There is little law business in Central Asia, and at this moment there was not enough to require a special agent in Australia. Carrington could hardly be induced to lead an expedition to the sources of the Nile in search of business merely to please Mr. Ratcliffe, nor could the State Department offer encouragement to a hope that government would pay the expenses of such an expedition. The best that Ratcliffe could do was to select the place of counsel to the Mexican claims-commission which was soon to meet in the city of Mexico, and which would require about six months’ absence. By a little management he could contrive to get the counsel sent away in advance of the commission, in order to work up a part of the case on the spot. Ratcliffe acknowledged that Mexico was too near, but he drily remarked to himself that if Carrington could get back in time to dislodge him after he had once got a firm hold on Mrs. Lee, he would never try to run another caucus.

===Chinese===
不出几日，拉特克利夫便又来看她——他正巴不得有这么个好由头。她把卡林顿的推辞说了，只添一句，他似乎不愿接受任何带政治色彩的职位。拉特克利夫毫无愠色，只以一副仁厚的口气说，不能为她这么好的朋友略效绵薄，深以为憾；如此一来，无论怎样，他在她的感激里总算立下了一笔债权。至于卡林顿，拉特克利夫开出的这份差事，本就没打算叫人接受；卡林顿若真的应下了，倒要把这位部长窘个够。拉特克利夫的目的，是把卡林顿的敌意这桩悬案，办成一桩让自己安心的定谳；因为他深知此人的为人，确信他无论如何都会行得堂堂正正。他若接受，至少会对长官尽忠；他若拒绝——正如拉特克利夫所料——便证明必须设法把他调开。无论哪一种，这份差事都是新的一根线，织进那张拉特克利夫自鸣得意、正飞快往李夫人的情丝与抱负上缠去的网。然而他自有理由认为，这张网的网眼，卡林顿若肯下手，比谁都容易铰开；因此，先将卡林顿料理停当，再图后举，方为上策。

他毫不耽搁，着手查访本部之外、政府所能支配的一切空缺或合宜的职位。合乎用途的寥寥无几。他要的是一桩临时性的法律差事，能把任职者带往一个远方待上一时——譬如澳大利亚，或者中亚，越远越好；俸禄必须丰厚，而且给的法子必须叫人疑不到拉特克利夫头上。这样的职位可不好找。中亚没什么法律业务，眼下澳大利亚那点事，也不够设一个专员。单为讨拉特克利夫先生的欢心，卡林顿总不至于肯领队去尼罗河源头寻找业务；国务院也断不能叫人指望，政府肯替这样一支远征队出资。拉特克利夫能选到的最好的，是即将在墨西哥城开审的墨西哥索赔委员会法律顾问一职，前后约需离家半年。稍加运筹，他还能安排这位顾问先委员会一步动身，就地先理出案情的一部分。拉特克利夫承认，墨西哥是近了些；可他暗自冷冷说道：倘若等他把李夫人牢牢抓在手里之后，卡林顿还赶得回来把他撬动，那他这辈子就再别想操办什么党团会议了。

===Original===
The point once settled in his own mind, Ratcliffe, with his usual rapidity of action, carried his scheme into effect. In this there was little difficulty. He dropped in at the office of the Secretary of State within eight-and-forty hours after his last conversation with Mrs. Lee. During these early days of every new administration, the absorbing business of government relates principally to appointments. The Secretary of the Treasury was always ready to oblige his colleagues in the Cabinet by taking care of their friends to any reasonable extent. The Secretary of State was not less courteous. The moment he understood that Mr. Ratcliffe had a strong wish to secure the appointment of a certain person as counsel to the Mexican claims-commission, the Secretary of State professed readiness to gratify him, and when he heard who the proposed person was, the suggestion was hailed with pleasure, for Carrington was well known and much liked at the Department, and was indeed an excellent man for the place. Ratcliffe hardly needed to promise an equivalent. The business was arranged in ten minutes.

===Chinese===
主意既定，拉特克利夫便以他惯常的雷厉风行，把算计落到了实处。这几乎不费什么周折。同李夫人末次谈话之后不出四十八个钟头，他就踱进了国务卿的办公室。每逢新政府开头的日子，政府的头等要务主要便是任免。财政部长一向乐得替内阁同僚照应朋友，凡在情理之中，无不可办。国务卿也不失礼数。他一听拉特克利夫先生切望某人出任墨西哥索赔委员会法律顾问，便慨然表示乐于成人之美；待听得拟任的是谁，这提议更受欢迎，因为卡林顿在国务院素有人望，极得人心，且确为此职的绝佳人选。拉特克利夫几乎无须许下什么对等的回报。事情十分钟便谈妥了。

===Original===
“I only need say,” added Ratcliffe, “that if my agency in the affair is known, Mr. Carrington will certainly refuse the place, for he is one of your old-fashioned Virginia planters, proud as Lucifer, and willing to accept nothing by way of favour. I will speak to your Assistant Secretary about it, and the recommendation shall appear to come from him.”

The very next day Carrington received a private note from his old friend, the Assistant Secretary of State, who was overjoyed to do him a kindness. The note asked him to call at the Department at his earliest convenience. He went, and the Assistant Secretary announced that he had recommended Carrington’s appointment as counsel to the Mexican claims-commission, and that the Secretary had approved the recommendation. “We want a Southern man, a lawyer with a little knowledge of international law, one who can go at once, and, above all, an honest man. You fit the description to a hair; so pack your trunk as soon as you like.”

===Chinese===
“我只需交代一句，”拉特克利夫补充道，“此事若有风声说与我相干，卡林顿先生必定拒绝就任；因为他是你们那种老派的弗吉尼亚种植园主，傲得赛过路西法，断不肯受人家半点恩惠。我去同贵部助理国务卿打个招呼，这道举荐，就当作出自他手了。”

第二天，卡林顿便收到老友、助理国务卿的一封私人便笺；这位正乐不可支，得了机会为他效一效劳。便笺请他得便尽早到部里一叙。他去了，助理国务卿宣布，他已举荐卡林顿出任墨西哥索赔委员会法律顾问，国务卿亦已核准。“我们要一位南方人，一位懂些国际法的律师，一位说走就能走的人，而顶要紧的，是一位诚实的人。你同这条件一根头发丝都不差；行李嘛，几时想收拾，几时就收拾。”

===Original===
Carrington was startled. Coming as it did, this offer was not only unobjectionable, but tempting. It was hard for him even to imagine a reason for hesitation. From the first he felt that he must go, and yet to go was the very last thing he wanted to do. That he should suspect Ratcliffe to be at the bottom of this scheme of banishment was a matter of course, and he instantly asked whether any influence had been used in his favour; but the Assistant Secretary so stoutly averred that the appointment was made on his recommendation alone, as to block all further inquiry. Technically this assertion was exact, and it made Carrington feel that it would be base ingratitude on his part not to accept a favour so handsomely offered.

Yet he could not make up his mind to acceptance. He begged four and twenty hours’ delay, in order, as he said, to see whether he could arrange his affairs for a six months’ absence, although he knew there would be no difficulty in his doing so. He went away and sat in his office alone, gloomily wondering what he could do, although from the first he saw that the situation was only too clear, and there could not be the least dark corner of a doubt to crawl into. Six months ago he would have jumped at this offer. What had happened within six months to make it seem a disaster?

===Chinese===
卡林顿吃了一惊。这份差事以这般来路送到，非但无可挑剔，反倒颇为诱人。要他想出一个迟疑的理由，都觉为难。打头一刻他便觉得非去不可，而去，又偏偏是他顶不愿意做的事。他自要疑心这场发配背后的主使是拉特克利夫，当即问可曾有什么人为他出过力；可助理国务卿一口咬定，此项任命纯出他本人的举荐，把进一步的追问尽数堵死了。就字面而论，这话分毫不差；这倒叫卡林顿觉得，如此体面奉上的好意若不肯领受，简直是忘恩负义。

然而他终究下不了应承的决心。他讨了二十四小时的宽限，说是要看看私事能否安排妥帖、好作半年之别——尽管他知道这毫无难处。他走开去，独自坐在办公室里，阴郁地盘算着何去何从，尽管他打一开始就看出，局面清楚得无以复加，连一丝可供疑心栖身的暗角也没有。换了六个月前，他会抢着抓牢这份差事。这六个月里出了什么事，竟把它变作一场灾祸？

===Original===
Mrs. Lee! There was the whole story. To go away now was to give up Mrs. Lee, and probably to give her up to Ratcliffe. Carrington gnashed his teeth when he thought how skilfully Ratcliffe was playing his cards. The longer he reflected, the more certain he felt that Ratcliffe was at the bottom of this scheme to get rid of him; and yet, as he studied the situation, it occurred to him that after all it was possible for Ratcliffe to make a blunder. This Illinois politician was clever, and understood men; but a knowledge of men is a very different thing from a knowledge of women. Carrington himself had no great experience in the article of women, but he thought he knew more than Ratcliffe, who was evidently relying most on his usual theory of political corruption as applied to feminine weaknesses, and who was only puzzled at finding how high a price Mrs. Lee set on herself. If Ratcliffe were really at the bottom of the scheme for separating Carrington from her, it could only be because he thought that six months, or even six weeks, would be enough to answer his purpose. And on reaching this point in his reflections, Carrington suddenly rose, lit a cigar, and walked up and down his room steadily for the next hour, with the air of a general arranging a plan of campaign, or a lawyer anticipating his opponent’s line of argument. On one point his mind was made up. He would accept. If Ratcliffe really had a hand in this move, he should be gratified. If he had laid a trap, he should be caught in it. And when the evening came, Carrington took his hat and walked off to call upon Mrs. Lee.

===Chinese===
李夫人！谜底尽在于此。此刻一走，便是撒手撒开李夫人，而且多半是把她撒给拉特克利夫。想到拉特克利夫的牌打得这般娴熟，卡林顿咬牙切齿。他越想越认定，这打发他走的算计，后台必是拉特克利夫；然而细细拆解局面，他又想到，拉特克利夫毕竟也有失手的可能。这位伊利诺伊政客精明，懂男人；可懂男人同懂女人，全然是两回事。论“女人”这一门，卡林顿自己也谈不上阅历深厚，但他自忖总比拉特克利夫多懂几分；拉特克利夫倚仗的，分明还是他那套政治收买的老理论，挪来套女人的软处，只是纳罕李夫人给自己的标价竟这等之高。倘若拆散卡林顿与她果真出于拉特克利夫的调度，那无非因为他认定，六个月，哪怕六个星期，便足以成事。想到这里，卡林顿霍然起身，点上一支雪茄，随后的整整一个钟头，不停地在屋里踱来踱去，那副神气，像一位部署战役的将军，又像一位预判对手论证路线的律师。有一节他已拿定主意：去。倘若这一着真有拉特克利夫的份，便由他得意去。倘若他设下的是陷阱，便教他自己落进去。到了晚间，卡林顿拿起帽子，往李夫人府上去了。

===Original===
He found the sisters alone and quietly engaged in their occupations. Madeleine was dramatically mending an openwork silk stocking, a delicate and difficult task which required her whole mind. Sybil was at the piano as usual, and for the first time since he had known her, she rose when he came in, and, taking her workbasket, sat down to share in the conversation. She meant to take her place as a woman, henceforward. She was tired of playing girl. Mr. Carrington should see that she was not a fool.

Carrington plunged at once into his subject, and announced the offer made to him, at which Madeleine expressed delight, and asked many questions. What was the pay? How soon must he go? How long should he be away? Was there danger from the climate? and finally she added, with a smile, “What am I to say to Mr. Ratcliffe if you accept this offer after refusing his?” As for Sybil, she made one reproachful exclamation: “Oh, Mr. Carrington!” and sank back into silence and consternation. Her first experiment at taking a stand of her own in the world was not encouraging. She felt betrayed.

===Chinese===
他见姐妹俩独自在家，正安静地各忙各的。马德琳正煞有介事地织补一双网眼丝袜，这活计又精细又吃功夫，须得她全副心神。西比尔照例坐在钢琴前；而自他认识她以来破天荒头一遭，他一进来，她竟站起身，拿起针线筐，坐下来加入谈话。她是打定了主意，从今往后，以一个女人的身份入席。扮小姑娘，她扮腻了。得叫卡林顿先生瞧瞧，她不是个傻瓜。

卡林顿开门见山讲明来意，宣布了送到他头上的这份差事；马德琳闻言大喜，连声发问。薪水几何？几时动身？离家多久？那边的气候可有什么凶险？末了她含笑添了一句：“你辞了拉特克利夫先生的差事，倒接下这一份，叫我怎么对他说呢？”至于西比尔，只发出一声嗔怪的惊叹：“哦，卡林顿先生！”便跌回沉默与惊愕里。她头一回尝试在这世上自拿一回主意，结果毫无鼓舞可言。她觉得被人出卖了。

===Original===
Nor was Carrington gay. However modest a man may be, only an idiot can forget himself entirely in pursuing the moon and the stars. In the bottom of his soul, he had a lingering hope that when he told his story, Madeleine might look up with a change of expression, a glance of unpremeditated regard, a little suffusion of the eyes, a little trembling of the voice. To see himself relegated to Mexico with such cheerful alacrity by the woman he loved was not the experience he would have chosen. He could not help feeling that his hopes were disposed of, and he watched her with a painful sinking of the heart, which did not lead to lightness of conversation. Madeleine herself felt that her expressions needed to be qualified, and she tried to correct her mistake. What should she do without a tutor? she said. He must let her have a list of books to read while he was away: they were themselves going north in the middle of May, and Carrington would be back by the time they returned in December. After all, they should see as little of him during the summer if he were in Virginia as if he were in Mexico.

===Chinese===
卡林顿也不快活。一个男人再怎么谦抑，追起月亮和星星来，也只有白痴才会把自己忘个干净。在他灵魂的底处，还存着一缕指望：待他把事情和盘托出，马德琳或许会抬眼之间神色一变，投来一瞥不假思索的垂青，眼里微微一润，声音微微一颤。眼见自己被所爱的女人这般喜滋滋、麻利利地发配去墨西哥，可不是他自己会挑的际遇。他不能不觉出，自己的指望已被发落了；他望着她，心口一阵阵往下沉，谈话自然也轻快不起来。马德琳自己也觉出话说得过了，赶忙设法补救。没了导师，她可怎么办？她说。他得给她开一张书单，供他不在时研读：她们自己五月中就要北上，等十二月回来，卡林顿也该回来了。说到底，夏天他若在弗吉尼亚，她们同他见面的机会，也同他在墨西哥一样少。

===Original===
Carrington gloomily confessed that he was very unwilling to go; that he wished the idea had never been suggested; that he should be perfectly happy if for any reason the scheme broke down; but he gave no explanation of his feeling, and Madeleine had too much tact to press for one. She contented herself by arguing against it, and talking as vivaciously as she could. Her heart really bled for him as she saw his face grow more and more pathetic in its quiet expression of disappointment. But what could she say or do? He sat till after ten o’clock; he could not tear himself away. He felt that this was the end of his pleasure in life; he dreaded the solitude of his thoughts. Mrs. Lee’s resources began to show signs of exhaustion. Long pauses intervened between her remarks; and at length Carrington, with a superhuman effort, apologized for inflicting himself upon her so unmercifully. If she knew, he said, how he dreaded being alone, she would forgive him. Then he rose to go, and, in taking leave, asked Sybil if she was inclined to ride the next day; if so, he was at her service. Sybil’s face brightened as she accepted the invitation.

Mrs. Lee, a day or two afterwards, did mention Carrington’s appointment to Mr. Ratcliffe, and she told Carrington that the Secretary certainly looked hurt and mortified, but showed it only by almost instantly changing the subject.

===Chinese===
卡林顿郁郁地招认，他极其不愿去；巴不得这个主意从没人提起过；这桩安排无论因什么缘故塌了台，他都会快活至极；但对于自己的心绪，他半句不解释，马德琳也乖觉得很，不肯相逼。她只管拿话劝阻，竭力说得活泼热闹。眼见他脸上那份安静的失望越来越楚楚可怜，她的心真是在为他滴血。可她还能说什么、做什么呢？他坐到十点钟过后，还是舍不得走。他觉得，这是他此生欢愉的尽头；他害怕自家思绪里的那份荒凉。李夫人的话头渐渐显出枯竭之相，话与话之间隔着长长的停顿；末了，卡林顿费了超人的力气，为自己这般狠命地缠磨她而告罪。她说，她若知道他多怕独处，便会原谅他的。于是他起身告辞；临别时问西比尔，明日可想骑马；若想，他随时奉陪。西比尔应下这邀约，脸上顿时放了光。

一两天后，李夫人果然向拉特克利夫先生提起了卡林顿的任命，又告诉卡林顿：部长确实露出了受创而羞恼的神色，只不过立时把话头岔开，不教它显露出来罢了。

## X / 十

===Original===
The next morning Carrington called at the Department and announced his acceptance of the post. He was told that his instructions would be ready in about a fortnight, and that he would be expected to start as soon as he received them; in the meanwhile, he must devote himself to the study of a mass of papers in the Department. There was no trifling allowable here. Carrington had to set himself vigorously to work.

===Chinese===
第二天上午，卡林顿到部里去，宣布接受那项任命。人家告诉他，训令大约两周后便可备妥，一领到便得动身；在此期间，他须专心研读部里的一大堆文件。这里可容不得半点敷衍。卡林顿只得振作精神，奋力投入工作。

===Original===
This did not, however, prevent him from keeping his appointment with Sybil, and at four o’clock they started together, passing out into the quiet shadows of Rock Creek, and seeking still lanes through the woods where their horses walked side by side, and they themselves could talk without the risk of criticism from curious eyes. It was the afternoon of one of those sultry and lowering spring days when life germinates rapidly, but as yet gives no sign, except perhaps some new leaf or flower pushing its soft head up against the dead leaves that have sheltered it. The two riders had something of the same sensation, as though the leafless woods and the laurel thickets, the warm, moist air and the low clouds, were a protection and a soft shelter. Somewhat to Carrington’s surprise, he found that it was pleasant to have Sybil’s company. He felt towards her as to a sister﻿—a favourite sister.
She at once attacked him for abandoning her and breaking his treaty so lately made, and he tried to gain her sympathy by saying that if she knew how much he was troubled, she would forgive him. Then when Sybil asked whether he really must go and leave her without any friend whom she could speak to, his feelings got the better of him: he could not resist the temptation to confide all his troubles in her, since there was no one else in whom he could confide. He told her plainly that he was in love with her sister.

===Chinese===
不过，这并不妨碍他如约去赴西比尔的约。四点钟，两人一同出发，出了城，走进罗克溪（Rock Creek）一带安静的树影，寻着林间僻静的小径，让两匹马并辔而行，他们自己也好畅谈，不怕被好奇的眼睛品评。那是一个闷热低沉的春日午后；这样的日子里，生命正在急速孕育，却还不露一点迹象，至多不过哪片新叶、哪朵新花，把柔软的头顶悄悄拱上庇护过它的枯叶。两个骑马的人多少怀着同样的感觉，仿佛那还未生叶的树林和月桂丛，那温湿的空气和低垂的云，都是一层屏障、一层温柔的荫蔽。卡林顿颇觉意外地发现，有西比尔作伴竟是件愉快的事。他对她怀着一种对妹妹的感情——而且是对最偏爱的妹妹的那种。
她立刻发起攻击，怪他抛弃了她，怪他这么快就撕毁了新订不久的盟约；他则想博取她的同情，说倘若她知道他心里有多少烦难，就会原谅他了。待到西比尔问他，是否当真非走不可，要把她撇在这里，连一个能说说话的朋友都不留，他的感情便再也把持不住：反正除了她再没有第二个人可以倾吐，他抵挡不住诱惑，把满腹心事统统托付给了她。他明白告诉她，他爱的是她姐姐。

===Original===
“You say that love is nonsense, Miss Ross. I tell you it is no such thing. For weeks and months it is a steady physical pain, an ache about the heart, never leaving one, by night or by day; a long strain on one’s nerves like toothache or rheumatism, not intolerable at any one instant, but exhausting by its steady drain on the strength. It is a disease to be borne with patience, like any other nervous complaint, and to be treated with counterirritants. My trip to Mexico will be good for it, but that is not the reason why I must go.”

===Chinese===
“您说爱情是无稽之谈，罗斯小姐。我告诉您，绝不是那么回事。一连几个星期、几个月，它是一种持续的肉体疼痛，一种心头的隐痛，日日夜夜缠着人不放；像牙痛或风湿一样，是对神经的长久磨折，任一瞬间都不至于忍无可忍，却靠着一丝一缕地耗损气力，把人拖垮。这是一种病，得像别的神经方面的毛病一样耐心忍受，用抗刺激剂来治。我这趟墨西哥之行对它会有好处，可那并不是我非走不可的缘由。”

===Original===
Then he told her all his private circumstances; the ruin which the war had brought on him and his family; how, of his two brothers, one had survived the war only to die at home, a mere wreck of disease, privation, and wounds; the other had been shot by his side, and bled slowly to death in his arms during the awful carnage in the Wilderness; how his mother and two sisters were struggling for a bare subsistence on a wretched Virginian farm, and how all his exertions barely kept them from beggary.

===Chinese===
接着，他把自家的私密境况和盘托出：战争怎样把他和他的家庭毁到了这步田地；他的两个兄弟，一个熬过了战争，却只落得回家死去，疾病、匮乏和创伤把人磨成了一副空架子；另一个在他身旁中了枪，在“荒野”（the Wilderness）那场骇人的大屠戮里，在他怀中慢慢地流尽了血；他的母亲和两个妹妹，怎样在弗吉尼亚一座贫瘠的农场上挣命糊口，而他怎样的拼命操劳，也才勉强使她们不至于沦为乞丐。

===Original===
“You have no conception of the poverty to which our southern women are reduced since the war,” said he; “they are many of them literally without clothes or bread.” The fee he should earn by going to Mexico would double his income this year. Could he refuse? Had he a right to refuse? And poor Carrington added, with a groan, that if he alone were in question, he would sooner be shot than go.

===Chinese===
“开战以来，我们南方的女人沦落到了怎样的穷困地步，您无从想象，”他说；“她们当中好些人，真真是连衣裳带面包都没有。”去墨西哥挣下的那笔酬金，能使他今年的进项翻上一倍。他能拒绝吗？他有权利拒绝吗？可怜的卡林顿又呻吟了一声，补上一句：倘若只关系到他一个人，他宁可挨枪子儿也不去。

===Original===
Sybil listened with tears in her eyes. She never before had seen a man show suffering. The misery she had known in life had been more or less veiled to her and softened by falling on older and friendly shoulders. She now got for the first time a clear view of Carrington, apart from the quiet exterior in which the man was hidden. She felt quite sure, by a sudden flash of feminine inspiration, that the curious look of patient endurance on his face was the work of a single night when he had held his brother in his arms, and knew that the blood was draining drop by drop from his side, in the dense, tangled woods, beyond the reach of help, hour after hour, till the voice failed and the limbs grew stiff and cold. When he had finished his story, she was afraid to speak. She did not know how to show her sympathy, and she could not bear to seem unsympathetic. In her embarrassment she fairly broke down and could only dry her eyes in silence.

===Chinese===
西比尔含着眼泪听。她从没见过一个男人把苦楚露在脸上。她平生知道的苦难，多多少少都替她遮着一层纱，都因为落到了年长的、亲近的肩上而变软了。此刻她头一回看清楚了卡林顿，看清了那副平静外表底下藏着的人。凭着一道女性直觉的灵光，她全然确信：他脸上那种坚忍得出的奇特神色，正是单独一个夜晚刻下的——那一夜，他把兄弟抱在怀里，眼看着血从那肋侧一滴一滴流干，在密不透风、荆棘纠缠的密林深处，呼救无门，一个钟头又一个钟头地挨着，直到嗓音消逝、四肢僵冷。他讲完自己的故事，她怕得不敢开口。她不知道该怎样表示同情，又受不了显得无情。窘迫之下，她索性垮了下来，只能默默拭干眼睛。

===Original===
Having once got this weight of confidence off his mind, Carrington felt comparatively gay and was ready to make the best of things. He laughed at himself to drive away the tears of his pretty companion, and obliged her to take a solemn pledge never to betray him. “Of course your sister knows it all,” he said; “but she must never know that I told you, and I never would tell anyone but you.”
Sybil promised faithfully to keep his confidence to herself, and she went on to defend her sister.

===Chinese===
这副重担一经卸下，卡林顿觉得比较轻快了，也准备往好里看了。他打趣自己，好赶走他这位漂亮同伴的眼泪，又逼着她立下庄严的誓约，永远不出卖他。“这些事您姐姐自然全都知道，”他说；“可她绝不能知道是我告诉您的，而且除了您，我也永远不会告诉任何人。”
西比尔信誓旦旦，答应替他把秘密守在心里，接着便替姐姐辩护起来。

===Original===
“You must not blame Madeleine,” said she; “if you knew as well as I do what she has been through, you would not think her cold. You do know how suddenly her husband died, after only one day’s illness, and what a nice fellow he was. She was very fond of him, and his death seemed to stun her. We hardly knew what to make of it, she was so quiet and natural. Then just a week later her little child died of diphtheria, suffering horribly, and she wild with despair because she could not relieve it. After that, she was almost insane; indeed, I have always thought she was quite insane for a time. I know she was excessively violent and wanted to kill herself, and I never heard anyone rave as she did about religion and resignation and God. After a few weeks she became quiet and stupid and went about like a machine; and at last she got over it, but has never been what she was before. You know she was a rather fast New York girl before she married, and cared no more about politics and philanthropy than I do. It was a very late thing, all this stuff. But she is not really hard, though she may seem so. It is all on the surface. I always know when she is thinking about her husband or child, because her face gets rigid; she looks then as she used to look after her child died, as though she didn’t care what became of her and she would just as lieve kill herself as not. I don’t think she will ever let herself love anyone again. She has a horror of it. She is much more likely to go in for ambition, or duty, or self-sacrifice.”

===Chinese===
“您可不能怪马德琳，”她说；“您要是同我一样清楚她经受过什么，就不会觉得她冷了。您也知道她丈夫死得多突然，只病了一天就没了，而他又是多好的一个人。她很爱他，他的死似乎把她打懵了。我们简直不知道该怎么看，她那么安静、那么自然。随后恰恰一个星期之后，她的小孩子得白喉死了，苦得吓人，她急得发了狂，因为没法替孩子受罪。打那以后，她几乎疯了；说真的，我一直认为她有一阵子是彻底疯了的。我知道她闹得凶极了，一心想寻死，她那样大嚷大叫地谈宗教、谈顺从、谈上帝，我从没听谁那样说过胡话。过了几个星期，她安静下来，变得木木痴痴，像个机器似的走来走去；末了总算熬了过去，可再也不是从前的她了。您知道，出嫁之前，她原是个相当轻狂的纽约姑娘，对政治和慈善的热心，同我不相上下——一点也没有。这一套全是后来的事。可她并不是真的硬心肠，虽然看着像。那都在表皮上。她什么时候在想她丈夫或孩子，我总知道，因为她的脸会发僵；那时她的神气，就像孩子死掉之后那阵子一样，仿佛不在乎自己会怎么样，寻死不寻死全无所谓。我想她再也不会让自己爱上谁了。她怕那个，怕得厉害。她更可能一头扎进野心、本分或者自我牺牲里去。”

===Original===
They rode on for a while in silence, Carrington perplexed by the problem how two harmless people such as Madeleine and he could have been made by a beneficent Providence the sport of such cruel tortures; and Sybil equally interested in thinking what sort of a brother-in-law Carrington would make; on the whole, she thought she liked him better as he was. The silence was only broken by Carrington’s bringing the conversation back to its starting-point: “Something must be done to keep your sister out of Ratcliffe’s power. I have thought about it till I am tired. Can you make no suggestion?”

===Chinese===
两人默默骑行了一程，卡林顿被一道难题缠住：像马德琳和他这样两个于人无害的人，怎么竟会被仁慈的上天当作玩物，受这样残酷的折磨；西比尔也同样起劲地盘算着，卡林顿若做了姐夫会是什么光景；总的说来，她觉得自己更喜欢他现在这样。沉默终于还是被卡林顿打破，他把话头拉回出发点：“总得想个法子，别让您姐姐落到拉特克利夫的掌心里。我想到都想累了。您就一点主意也提不出来吗？”

===Original===
No! Sybil was helpless and dreadfully alarmed. Mr. Ratcliffe came to the house as often as he could, and seemed to tell Madeleine everything that was going on in politics, and ask her advice, and Madeleine did not discourage him. “I do believe she likes it, and thinks she can do some good by it. I don’t dare speak to her about it. She thinks me a child still, and treats me as though I were fifteen. What can I do?”

===Chinese===
提不出来！西比尔一筹莫展，又怕得要命。拉特克利夫先生一得空就往宅子里跑，政界正在发生的一切似乎都讲给马德琳听，还向她讨主意，而马德琳也不曾冷落他。“我确信她喜欢这样，还觉得自己能借此做点好事。这话我不敢同她提。她至今把我当孩子，把我当十五岁的人待。我能有什么办法呢？”

===Original===
Carrington said he had thought of speaking to Mrs. Lee himself, but he did not know what to say, and if he offended her, he might drive her directly into Ratcliffe’s arms. But Sybil thought she would not be offended if he went to work in the right way. “She will stand more from you than from anyone else. Tell her openly that you﻿—that you love her,” said Sybil with a burst of desperate courage; “she can’t take offence at that; and then you can say almost anything.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿说，他也想过亲自去同李夫人谈，可又不知说什么好，万一开罪了她，倒可能把她径直推进拉特克利夫的怀抱。但西比尔认为，只要他路数走得对，她不会见怪的。“旁人说的话她受不了的，从您嘴里说出来她都能受。您就大大方方地告诉她，您——您爱她，”西比尔鼓起一股豁出去的勇气说；“这个她没法见怪；往后您差不多说什么都行了。”

===Original===
Carrington looked at Sybil with more admiration than he had ever expected to feel for her, and began to think that he might do worse than to put himself under her orders. After all, she had some practical sense, and what was more to the point, she was handsomer than ever, as she sat erect on her horse, the rich colour rushing up under the warm skin, at the impropriety of her speech. “You are certainly right,” said he; “after all, I have nothing to lose. Whether she marries Ratcliffe or not, she will never marry me, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿望着西比尔，心里涌起的钦佩连他自己都未曾料到，并且开始觉得，把自己交给她调遣，倒也不失为一个不坏的主意。毕竟她有些务实的见识，更要紧的是，她比什么时候都标致——她挺直地坐在马上，为自己这话的失体统，浓浓的血色从温热的皮肤底下涌了上来。“您说得对，”他说；“说到底，我没什么可输的。她嫁不嫁拉特克利夫，反正永远不会嫁我，我料着是这样。”

===Original===
This speech was a cowardly attempt to beg encouragement from Sybil, and met with the fate it deserved, for Sybil, highly flattered at Carrington’s implied praise, and bold as a lioness now that it was Carrington’s fingers, and not her own, that were to go into the fire, gave him on the spot a feminine view of the situation that did not encourage his hopes. She plainly said that men seemed to take leave of their senses as soon as women were concerned; for her part, she could not understand what there was in any woman to make such a fuss about; she thought most women were horrid; men were ever so much nicer; “and as for Madeleine, whom all of you are ready to cut each other’s throats about, she’s a dear, good sister, as good as gold, and I love her with all my heart, but you wouldn’t like her, any of you, if you married her; she has always had her own way, and she could not help taking it; she never could learn to take yours; both of you would be unhappy in a week; and as for that old Mr. Ratcliffe, she would make his life a burden﻿—and I hope she will,” concluded Sybil with a spiteful little explosion of hatred.

===Chinese===
这番话原是怯懦地想从西比尔那里讨一点鼓励，也得到了它应得的报应：西比尔被卡林顿话里暗含的夸赞捧得心花怒放，如今要伸进火里去的是卡林顿的手指头、不是她自己的，便勇猛得像头母狮，当场给了他一套女人家的时局之见，毫不给他指望。她明明白白地说，男人一沾上女人的边，似乎就神魂颠倒；就她而论，她实在不明白女人身上到底有什么，值得这样大惊小怪；她认为大多数女人都可怕得很；男人可要好得多；“至于马德琳，你们一个个都恨不得为着她割断彼此的喉咙，她是个亲爱的好姐姐，好得像金子，我也一心一意爱她；可你们谁要是娶了她，谁就不会喜欢她了；她从来是自己要怎样就怎样，想不这样也难；她永远学不会依着你们的性子；不出一个礼拜，你们两个都要痛苦；至于那位拉特克利夫老先生，她准会把他的日子变成一种苦刑——我还巴望她这么干，”西比尔末了带着一星怨毒的火气，发作了一句，收住了话头。

===Original===
Carrington could not help being amused by Sybil’s way of dealing with affairs of the heart. Emboldened by encouragement, she went on to attack him pitilessly for going down on his knees before her sister, “just as though you were not as good as she is,” and openly avowed that, if she were a man, she would at least have some pride. Men like this kind of punishment. Carrington did not attempt to defend himself; he even courted Sybil’s attack. They both enjoyed their ride through the bare woods, by the rippling spring streams, under the languid breath of the moist south wind. It was a small idyll, all the more pleasant because there was gloom before and behind it. Sybil’s irrepressible gaiety made Carrington doubt whether, after all, life need be so serious a matter. She had animal spirits in plenty, and it needed an effort for her to keep them down, while Carrington’s spirits were nearly exhausted after twenty years of strain, and he required a greater effort to hold himself up. There was every reason why he should be grateful to Sybil for lending to him from her superfluity. He enjoyed being laughed at by her. Suppose Madeleine Lee did refuse to marry him! What of it? “Pooh!” said Sybil; “you men are all just alike. How can you be so silly? Madeleine and you would be intolerable together. Do find someone who won’t be solemn!”

===Chinese===
西比尔对付心头之事的这套路数，叫卡林顿忍不住觉得有趣。她得了鼓励，胆子更壮，继续毫不留情地攻击他跪倒在她姐姐面前，“说得好像您就不如她似的”，还公然声言，她若是个男人，好歹也存几分傲气。男人就吃这一套责罚。卡林顿并不企图自辩；他简直是有意招惹西比尔的攻击。两人一路骑行，穿过光秃秃的树林，傍着淙淙作响的春溪，沐着湿南风慵懒的气息，都觉愉快。这是一小段田园诗，格外宜人，因为前前后后都罩着阴霾。西比尔那股压不住的欢快，叫卡林顿怀疑起来：人生到底是否真需要这般严肃。她的血气精神绰绰有余，要压下去还得费一番劲；卡林顿的精气神，经二十年劳顿已近耗竭，要撑住自己得费更大的劲。所以他满有理由感激西比尔，感激她从自己的盈余里匀出一份借给他。他喜欢被她取笑。就算马德琳·李当真不肯嫁给他，又怎样呢！“呸！”西比尔说；“你们男人全是一个样。怎么能这样傻？马德琳同您凑成一对，谁也受不了。快去找个不板面孔的吧！”

===Original===
They laid out their little plot against Madeleine and elaborated it carefully, both as to what Carrington should say and how he should say it, for Sybil asserted that men were too stupid to be trusted even in making a declaration of love, and must be taught, like little children to say their prayers. Carrington enjoyed being taught how to make a declaration of love. He did not ask where Sybil had learned so much about men’s stupidity. He thought perhaps Schneidekoupon could have thrown light on the subject. At all events, they were so busily occupied with their schemes and lessons, that they did not reach home till Madeleine had become anxious lest they had met with some accident. The long dusk had become darkness before she heard the clatter of hoofs on the asphalt pavement, and she went down to the door to scold them for their delay. Sybil only laughed at her, and said it was all Mr. Carrington’s fault: he had lost his way, and she had been forced to find it for him.

===Chinese===
他们把这桩对付马德琳的小小计谋铺排开来，细细推敲：卡林顿该说什么，又该怎么说，都商量到了；因为西比尔断言，男人蠢得很，连表白爱情这样的事都不放心交给他们，得像教小孩子念祷告那样，一句一句地教。卡林顿很享受学怎样表白爱情。他没有问，西比尔从哪里学到这么多关于男人之蠢的学问。他想，这个题目，施奈德库彭先生或许能点拨一二。无论如何，两人忙着计谋和功课，竟忙到马德琳已担心他们路上出了意外，才回到家来。漫长的暮色早已黑透，她才听见马蹄踏在沥青路面上的嘚嘚声，便下楼到门口，数落他们回来得这样晚。西比尔只冲她笑，说全是卡林顿先生的错：他迷了路，倒要她替他把路找回来。

===Original===
Ten days more passed before their plan was carried into effect. April had come. Carrington’s work was completed and he was ready to start on his journey. Then at last he appeared one evening at Mrs. Lee’s at the very moment when Sybil, as chance would have it, was going out to pass an hour or two with her friend Victoria Dare a few doors away. Carrington felt a little ashamed as she went. This kind of conspiracy behind Mrs. Lee’s back was not to his taste.

===Chinese===
又过了十天，他们的计划才付诸实施。四月到了。卡林顿的公务已经办完，随时可以动身。终于，一天傍晚，他来到李夫人家，偏巧那时西比尔正要出门，去隔几户人家、她的朋友维多利亚·戴尔那里消磨一两个钟头。她走的时候，卡林顿心里有点惭愧。这种背着李夫人的密谋，不合他的口味。

===Original===
He resolutely sat down, and plunged at once into his subject. He was almost ready to go, he said; he had nearly completed his work in the Department, and he was assured that his instructions and papers would be ready in two days more; he might not have another chance to see Mrs. Lee so quietly again, and he wanted to take his leave now, for this was what lay most heavily on his mind; he should have gone willingly and gladly if it had not been for uneasiness about her; and yet he had till now been afraid to speak openly on the subject. Here he paused for a moment as though to invite some reply.

===Chinese===
他毅然坐下，立刻切入正题。他说，他差不多可以动身了；部里的事已近办完，人家向他保证，再过两天训令和文书就能备齐；往后未必再有像这样安安静静见李夫人的机会，他要现在就来辞行，因为这是他心头压得最重的一件事；若不是为了对她放心不下，他本会走得心甘情愿；然而直到此刻，他一直不敢把这层意思明说。说到这里他停了片刻，仿佛在请她接话。

===Original===
Madeleine laid down her work with a look of regret though not of annoyance, and said frankly and instantly that he had been too good a friend to allow of her taking offence at anything he could say; she would not pretend to misunderstand him. “My affairs,” she added with a shade of bitterness, “seem to have become public property, and I would rather have some voice in discussing them myself than to know they are discussed behind my back.”
This was a sharp thrust at the very outset, but Carrington turned it aside and went quietly on:

===Chinese===
马德琳放下手里的活计，神情带着惋惜，却并无愠色，即刻坦率地说，他素来是太好的朋友，无论他说什么，都谈不到见怪；她也不愿装作误会他。“我的事，”她微带苦涩地补了一句，“似乎已成一桩公共产业，与其知道人家在背后议论，我倒宁愿自己也有一句话可说，亲自参加讨论。”
这话劈头就是一记尖利的突刺，卡林顿却把它挡了开去，不动声色地往下说：

===Original===
“You are frank and loyal, as you always are. I will be so too. I can’t help being so. For months I have had no other pleasure than in being near you. For the first time in my life I have known what it is to forget my own affairs in loving a woman who seems to me without a fault, and for one solitary word from whom I would give all I have in life, and perhaps itself.”

===Chinese===
“你是坦率的、真诚的，一贯如此。我也要这样。我忍不住不这样。几个月来，我唯一的乐趣便是在你身边。平生第一次，我懂得了什么叫在爱着一个女人的时候忘掉自己的事——这个女人在我看来毫无瑕疵，为了她孤零零的一个字，我情愿献出此生所有的一切，也许连同生命本身。”

===Original===
Madeleine flushed and bent towards him with an earnestness of manner that repeated itself in her tone:
“Mr. Carrington, I am the best friend you have on earth. One of these days you will thank me with your whole soul for refusing to listen to you now. You do not know how much misery I am saving you. I have no heart to give. You want a young, fresh life to help yours; a gay, lively temperament to enliven your despondency; someone still young enough to absorb herself in you and make all her existence yours. I could not do it. I can give you nothing. I have done my best to persuade myself that some day I might begin life again with the old hopes and feelings, but it is no use. The fire is burned out. If you married me, you would destroy yourself. You would wake up some day, and find the universe dust and ashes.”

===Chinese===
马德琳红了脸，朝他俯过身去，那副恳切的神情又在她的语声里重现出来：
“卡林顿先生，我是你在世上最好的朋友。将来有一天，你会全心全意感激我此刻不肯听你说下去。你不知道我这是在替你免去多少苦楚。我没有一颗心可以给人了。你要的是一段年轻新鲜的生命，来扶助你的生命；一个欢快活泼的性子，来振作你的消沉；一个还年轻得足以把自己整个沉浸到你身上、把自己全部的生存都变成你的人。这个我做不到。我什么也不能给你。我曾竭力说服自己，说不定有朝一日还能带着旧日的希望和感情重新开始生活，可是没有用。火已经烧尽了。你若娶了我，就是毁掉你自己。有一天你一觉醒来，会发现整个宇宙都成了尘土和灰烬。”

===Original===
Carrington listened in silence. He made no attempt to interrupt or to contradict her. Only at the end he said with a little bitterness: “My own life is worth so much to the world and to me, that I suppose it would be wrong to risk it on such a venture; but I would risk it, nevertheless, if you gave me the chance. Do you think me wicked for tempting Providence? I do not mean to annoy you with entreaties. I have a little pride left, and a great deal of respect for you. Yet I think, in spite of all you have said or can say, that one disappointed life may be as able to find happiness and repose in another, as to get them by sucking the young lifeblood of a fresh soul.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿默然听着。他没有试图打断她，也没有试图驳她。只在末了，他略带苦涩地说：“我这条命，对世界、对我自己都这样金贵，拿它去冒这样一场险，想必是不应该的；可只要你给我这个机会，我还是要冒。你觉得我罪过吗，竟敢试探天意？我不打算用苦苦哀求来烦扰你。我还剩下一点自尊，对你更有许多的敬重。然而我想，任凭你说过什么、还能说什么，一个失意的人生，要从另一个失意的人生里寻得幸福与安宁，同靠吮吸一个新鲜灵魂的青春血气来获取它们，本事原是一样大的。”

===Original===
To this speech, which was unusually figurative for Carrington, Mrs. Lee could find no ready answer. She could only reply that Carrington’s life was worth quite as much as his neighbour’s, and that it was worth so much to her, if not to himself, that she would not let him wreck it.

===Chinese===
这番话对卡林顿而言辞藻未免过甚，李夫人一时找不出现成的答复。她只能说，卡林顿这条命的分量，同他左近任何人的不相上下；而且就算对他自己不算什么，对她却够贵重的了，她不能眼看着他把这条命毁掉。

===Original===
Carrington went on: “Forgive my talking in this way. I do not mean to complain. I shall always love you just as much, whether you care for me or not, because you are the only woman I have ever met, or am ever likely to meet, who seems to me perfect.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿接着说：“原谅我这样说话。我不是要抱怨。往后我会永远同样地爱你，不管你在不在乎我，因为你是我平生遇到的、也是此生有望遇到的唯一一个，在我眼里近于完美的女人。”

===Original===
If this was Sybil’s teaching, she had made the best of her time. Carrington’s tone and words pierced through all Mrs. Lee’s armour as though they were pointed with the most ingenious cruelty, and designed to torture her. She felt hard and small before him. Life for life, his had been, and was now, far less bright than hers, yet he was her superior. He sat there, a true man, carrying his burden calmly, quietly, without complaint, ready to face the next shock of life with the same endurance he had shown against the rest. And he thought her perfect! She felt humiliated that any brave man should say to her face that he thought her perfect! She! perfect! In her contrition she was half ready to go down at his feet and confess her sins; her hysterical dread of sorrow and suffering, her narrow sympathies, her feeble faith, her miserable selfishness, her abject cowardice. Every nerve in her body tingled with shame when she thought what a miserable fraud she was; what a mass of pretensions unfounded, of deceit ingrained. She was ready to hide her face in her hands. She was disgusted, outraged with her own image as she saw it, contrasted with Carrington’s single word: Perfect!

===Chinese===
这些话若是西比尔教的，那她真把工夫用到了家。卡林顿的声调和字句，穿透了李夫人周身的甲胄，仿佛锋尖上淬着最精巧的残忍，专为折磨她而打造。她在他面前觉得自己又硬又小。就生活对生活，他的生活过去和现在都远不如她的明亮，他却比她高。他坐在那里，一个真正的男人，平静地、不出一声地扛着自己的重担，没有一句怨言，准备以他一路表现出的同样坚忍，去迎接生活的下一次打击。而他竟觉得她完美！任何一个勇敢的男人，竟当着她的面说他觉得她完美，这叫她感到屈辱！她！完美！在悔恨里，她几乎要跪倒在他脚前，忏悔自己的罪：她对悲哀与苦难歇斯底里的恐惧，她狭隘的同情，她薄弱的信念，她可怜的自私，她卑贱的懦怯。一想到自己是个多么可悲的赝品——一大堆毫无根由的矫饰、一身积习成性的伪诈——她周身每一根神经都羞得发麻。她直想把脸埋进双手里去。她看见的自己这副形象，同卡林顿那一个字两相对照——完美！——叫她又厌恶，又愤慨。

===Original===
Nor was this the worst. Carrington was not the first man who had thought her perfect. To hear this word suddenly used again, which had never been uttered to her before except by lips now dead and gone, made her brain reel. She seemed to hear her husband once more telling her that she was perfect. Yet against this torture, she had a better defence. She had long since hardened herself to bear these recollections, and they steadied and strengthened her. She had been called perfect before now, and what had come of it? Two graves, and a broken life! She drew herself up with a face now grown quite pale and rigid. In reply to Carrington, she said not a word, but only shook her head slightly without looking at him.

===Chinese===
这还不是最坏的。卡林顿不是第一个觉得她完美的男人。这个字忽然又被用到她身上——从前对她说出这个字的，只有一双如今已死已逝的嘴唇——她的头脑晕眩起来。她仿佛又听见她的丈夫在告诉她，她是完美的。然而对这一重酷刑，她有一道更好的防线。她早已把自己磨炼硬了，受得住这些回忆，它们反而使她站稳、使她坚强。她从前就被称作完美过，结果落了什么？两座坟，一段破碎的人生！她挺直了身子，脸色这时已变得十分苍白僵硬。对卡林顿，她一个字也没有回答，只微微摇了摇头，眼睛并不看他。

===Original===
He went on: “After all, it is not my own happiness I am thinking of but yours. I never was vain enough to think that I was worth your love, or that I could ever win it. Your happiness is another thing. I care so much for that as to make me dread going away, for fear that you may yet find yourself entangled in this wretched political life here, when, perhaps if I stayed, I might be of some use.”

===Chinese===
他接着说：“说到底，我想的不是我自己的幸福，而是你的。我从不曾自负到以为自己配得上你的爱，或以为能赢得它。你的幸福却是另一回事。我对它牵挂到这般地步，以致害怕离去，惟恐你终会发现自己缠陷进这里这种可怜的政治生活里去，而倘若我留下，说不定还能有点用处。”

===Original===
“Do you really think, then, that I am going to fall a victim to Mr. Ratcliffe?” asked Madeleine, with a cold smile.
“Why not?” replied Carrington, in a similar tone. “He can put forward a strong claim to your sympathy and help, if not to your love. He can offer you a great field of usefulness which you want. He has been very faithful to you. Are you quite sure that even now you can refuse him without his complaining that you have trifled with him?”

===Chinese===
“那么，你当真以为，我会做了拉特克利夫先生的牺牲品吗？”马德琳问，带着冷淡的微笑。
“为什么不会？”卡林顿用同样的声口答道。“即使够不上你的爱，他也能提出一条有力的要求，索取你的同情和帮助。他能提供给你一片你正想要的、大可施展所用的天地。他对你一直很忠实。你能否十分肯定，事到如今你拒绝他，他不至抱怨你玩弄了他？”

===Original===
“And are you quite sure,” added Mrs. Lee, evasively, “that you have not been judging him much too harshly? I think I know him better than you. He has many good qualities, and some high ones. What harm can he do me? Supposing even that he did succeed in persuading me that my life could be best used in helping his, why should I be afraid of it?”

===Chinese===
“那你又能否十分肯定，”李夫人躲躲闪闪地接道，“你评判他，没有苛刻得过了头？我想我比你更了解他。他有许多好品质，还有几样高贵的。他能害我什么？退一步说，就算他当真说动了我，叫我相信我的生命用来辅佐他最有价值，我又有什么可怕的？”

===Original===
“You and I,” said Carrington, “are wide apart in our estimates of Mr. Ratcliffe. To you, of course, he shows his best side. He is on his good behaviour, and knows that any false step will ruin him. I see in him only a coarse, selfish, unprincipled politician, who would either drag you down to his own level, or, what is more likely, would very soon disgust you and make your life a wretched self-immolation before his vulgar ambition, or compel you to leave him. In either case you would be the victim. You cannot afford to make another false start in life. Reject me! I have not a word to say against it. But be on your guard against giving your existence up to him.”

===Chinese===
“你和我，”卡林顿说，“对拉特克利夫先生的估价相去悬殊。在你面前，他当然拿出的是最好的一面。他正检点着言行，知道一步走岔便全盘皆毁。在我眼里，他只是一个粗鄙、自私、没有原则的政客：他要么把你拖到跟他一样的地步，要么——这更可能——很快就叫你生出厌恶，把你的一生变成为他庸俗的野心所作的一场可怜的自我牺牲，再不然就是逼得你离开他。无论哪种情形，你都是牺牲品。你再经不起把人生走错一步了。拒绝我吧！这话我没有半个字可说。只是千万提防，别把你的整个生存交付给他。”

===Original===
“Why do you think so ill of Mr. Ratcliffe?” asked Madeleine; “he always speaks highly of you. Do you know anything against him that the world does not?”
“His public acts are enough to satisfy me,” replied Carrington, evading a part of the question. “You know that I have never had but one opinion about him.”

===Chinese===
“你为什么把拉特克利夫先生想得这样坏？”马德琳问；“他谈起你，向来都是称许的。你可知道什么世人都不知道的、于他不利的事？”
“他的公开行径，尽够叫我信服了，”卡林顿答道，避开了问题的一部分。“你知道，我对他的看法，从来只有一种。”

===Original===
There was a pause in the conversation. Both parties felt that as yet no good had come of it. At length Madeleine asked, “What would you have me do? Is it a pledge you want that I will under no circumstances marry Mr. Ratcliffe?”

===Chinese===
谈话停顿了片刻。双方都感到，到现在为止还没谈出什么好处来。末了马德琳问：“你要我怎样呢？你要的，是一个保证吗——保证我无论如何都不嫁给拉特克利夫先生？”

===Original===
“Certainly not,” was the answer; “you know me better than to think I would ask that. I only want you to take time and keep out of his influence until your mind is fairly made up. A year hence I feel certain that you will think of him as I do.”
“Then you will allow me to marry him if I find that you are mistaken,” said Mrs. Lee, with a marked tone of sarcasm.

===Chinese===
“当然不是，”对方这样回答；“你了解我，不至于以为我会要求这个。我只要你给自己留出时间，躲开他的影响，直到你的心思真正拿定。一年之后，我深信你再看他的想法，就会同我一样。”
“那么，倘若我发现错的是你，你就许我嫁他了，”李夫人说，话音里带着分明可辨的讥讽。

===Original===
Carrington looked annoyed, but he answered quietly, “What I fear is his influence here and now. What I would like to see you do is this: go north a month earlier than you intended, and without giving him time to act. If I were sure you were safely in Newport, I should feel no anxiety.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿面有愠色，但他平静地答道：“我怕的是他此时此地的影响。我盼你做的是这样：比原定早一个月北上，不给他施展的时间。我若能确定你已安然置身纽波特，便再没有不放心的了。”

===Original===
“You seem to have as bad an opinion of Washington as Mr. Gore,” said Madeleine, with a contemptuous smile. “He gave me the same advice, though he was afraid to tell me why. I am not a child. I am thirty years old, and have seen something of the world. I am not afraid, like Mr. Gore, of Washington malaria, or, like you, of Mr. Ratcliffe’s influence. If I fall a victim I shall deserve my fate, and certainly I shall have no cause to complain of my friends. They have given me advice enough for a lifetime.”

===Chinese===
“你对华盛顿的看法，看来同戈尔先生一样糟，”马德琳说，带着轻蔑的微笑。“他给过我一模一样的劝告，只是不敢对我说出缘故。我不是孩子。我三十岁了，也见过些世面。我不像戈尔先生那样怕华盛顿的瘴气，也不像你这样怕拉特克利夫先生的影响。我若真做了牺牲品，那也是罪有应得，而且我的朋友们，我肯定没有什么好抱怨的。他们给我的忠告，够一辈子用的了。”

===Original===
Carrington’s face darkened with a deeper shade of regret. The turn which the conversation had taken was precisely what he had expected, and both Sybil and he had agreed that Madeleine would probably answer just in this way. Nevertheless, he could not but feel acutely the harm he was doing to his own interests, and it was only by a sheer effort of the will that he forced himself to a last and more earnest attack.

===Chinese===
卡林顿的脸色阴沉下来，添了一层更深的怅憾。谈话转到的这个方向，正是他预料中的，他和西比尔也曾一致断定，马德琳多半会这样回答。尽管如此，他仍不能不痛切地感到，他这是在损害自己的利益；只是凭着一股纯粹的意志力，他才强逼着自己发起最后一次、也更恳切的进攻。

===Original===
“I know it is an impertinence,” he said; “I wish it were in my power to show how much it costs me to offend you. This is the first time you ever had occasion to be offended. If I were to yield to the fear of your anger and were to hold my tongue now, and by any chance you were to wreck your life on this rock, I should never forgive myself the cowardice. I should always think I might have done something to prevent it. This is probably the last time I shall have the chance to talk openly with you, and I implore you to listen to me. I want nothing for myself. If I knew I should never see you again, I would still say the same thing. Leave Washington! Leave it now!﻿—at once!﻿—without giving more than twenty-four hours’ notice! Leave it without letting Mr. Ratcliffe see you again in private! Come back next winter if you please, and then accept him if you think proper. I only pray you to think long about it and decide when you are not here.”

===Chinese===
“我知道这是放肆，”他说；“但愿我有法子让你看出，开罪于你使我付出多大的代价。这是你头一回有动怒的缘由。我若屈服于对你的怒火的畏惧，此刻缄口不言，而你偏巧在这块礁石上撞碎了人生，我永远不能原谅自己的怯懦。我会永远想着，我本来也许能做点什么，防住这场祸事。这多半是我最后一次有机会同你开诚布公地谈话了，我恳求你听我说完。我自己一无所求。就算我知道从此再见不着你，我还是要说同样的话。离开华盛顿！现在就离开！——立刻！——至多留出二十四小时的通知！走的时候，别让拉特克利夫先生再单独见到你！你高兴的话，明年冬天再回来，那时你觉得合适，就答应他。我只求你把这件事翻来覆去想个透，等不在华盛顿的时候再作决定。”

===Original===
Madeleine’s eyes flashed, and she threw aside her embroidery with an impatient gesture: “No! Mr. Carrington! I will not be dictated to! I will carry out my own plans! I do not mean to marry Mr. Ratcliffe. If I had meant it, I should have done it before now. But I will not run away from him or from myself. It would be unladylike, undignified, cowardly.”

===Chinese===
马德琳双眼闪光，不耐烦地一挥手，把刺绣撂在一边：“不！卡林顿先生！我不受人摆布！我要照我自己的计划行事！我没有要嫁给拉特克利夫先生的意思。我若有意，早就嫁了。可我不逃避他，也不逃避我自己。那样做有失淑女身份，有失体统，是懦怯的。”

===Original===
Carrington could say no more. He had come to the end of his lesson. A long silence ensued and then he rose to go. “Are you angry with me?” said she in a softer tone.
“I ought to ask that question,” said he. “Can you forgive me? I am afraid not. No man can say to a woman what I have said to you, and be quite forgiven. You will never think of me again as you would have done if I had not spoken. I knew that before I did it. As for me, I can only go on with my old life. It is not gay, and will not be the gayer for our talk tonight.”

===Chinese===
卡林顿再没有可说的了。他的功课到了头。接着是长久的沉默，随后他起身告辞。“你生我的气了吗？”她换了一种柔和些的声调问。
“该问这句话的是我，”他说。“你能原谅我吗？我怕是不能了。一个男人对一个女人说出了我对你说出的这些话，就再不能被全然原谅。从此你再想起我，再不会是我不说这些话时你本会有的那种想法了。开口之前我就明白这一层。至于我，只能照旧过我从前的日子。那日子并不快活，经过今晚这一谈，也不会更快活。”

===Original===
Madeleine relented a little: “Friendships like ours are not so easily broken,” she said. “Do not do me another injustice. You will see me again before you go?”
He assented and bade good night. Mrs. Lee, weary and disturbed in mind, hastened to her room. “When Miss Sybil comes in, tell her that I am not very well, and have gone to bed,” were her instructions to her maid, and Sybil thought she knew the cause of this headache.

===Chinese===
马德琳缓和了一点：“像我们这样的交情，不是那么容易破裂的，”她说。“别再错怪我一回。你动身之前，还会再见我一面的吧？”
他应承了，道过晚安。李夫人身心疲惫，心绪纷乱，急忙回自己房里去。“西比尔小姐回来，告诉她我不大舒服，已经睡了，”这是她吩咐女仆的话；而西比尔自以为，她知道这场头疼的来由。

===Original===
But before Carrington’s departure he had one more ride with Sybil, and reported to her the result of the interview, at which both of them confessed themselves much depressed. Carrington expressed some hope that Madeleine meant, after a sort, to give a kind of pledge by saying that she had no intention of marrying Mr. Ratcliffe, but Sybil shook her head emphatically: “How can a woman tell whether she is going to accept a man until she is asked?” said she with entire confidence, as though she were stating the simplest fact in the world. Carrington looked puzzled, and ventured to ask whether women did not generally make up their minds beforehand on such an interesting point; but Sybil overwhelmed him with contempt: “What good will they do by making up their minds, I should like to know? of course they would go and do the opposite. Sensible women don’t pretend to make up their minds, Mr. Carrington. But you men are so stupid, and you can’t understand in the least.”

===Chinese===
但在卡林顿动身之前，他又同西比尔骑了一回马，向她报告了那场会面的结果，两人都承认心里大为沮丧。卡林顿还存着几分指望，说马德琳说她无意嫁给拉特克利夫先生，多少算是变着法子给了某种保证；西比尔却断然摇头：“一个女人，人家还没开口，她怎么知道自己是应还是不应呢？”她说得满怀自信，仿佛陈述的是天下最浅显的事实。卡林顿现出困惑的神色，大着胆子问，在这样有趣的一点上，女人是否通常都预先拿定了主意；西比尔却劈头盖脸回敬他一顿轻蔑：“我倒要请问，预先拿定主意又有什么用？她们当然要反着来。明白事理的女人，从来不装作拿定了主意，卡林顿先生。可你们男人蠢成这样，一丝一毫也不懂。”

===Original===
Carrington gave it up, and went back to his stale question: Could Sybil suggest any other resource? and Sybil sadly confessed that she could not. So far as she could see, they must trust to luck, and she thought it was cruel tor Mr. Carrington to go away and leave her alone without help. He had promised to prevent the marriage.

===Chinese===
卡林顿只得作罢，又回到他那问老了的问题：西比尔还能想出别的法子吗？西比尔怏怏地承认，想不出。照她看，他们只有听天由命；而卡林顿先生竟要一走了之，撇下她孤零零一个没有帮手，她认为这未免残忍。他可是答应过要阻止这桩婚事的。

===Original===
“One thing more I mean to do,” said Carrington: “and here everything will depend on your courage and nerve. You may depend upon it that Mr. Ratcliffe will offer himself before you go north. He does not suspect you of making trouble, and he will not think about you in any way if you let him alone and keep quiet. When he does offer himself you will know it; at least your sister will tell you if she has accepted him. If she refuses him point blank, you will have nothing to do but to keep her steady. If you see her hesitating, you must break in at any cost, and use all your influence to stop her. Be bold, then, and do your best. If everything fails and she still clings to him, I must play my last card, or rather you must play it for me. I shall leave with you a sealed letter which you are to give her if everything else fails. Do it before she sees Ratcliffe a second time. See that she reads it and, if necessary, make her read it, no matter when or where. No one else must know that it exists, and you must take as much care of it as though it were a diamond. You are not to know what is in it; it must be a complete secret. Do you understand?”

===Chinese===
“还有一件事我打算做，”卡林顿说：“而这件事，全看您的胆量和沉勇了。您尽可以相信，赶在你们北上之前，拉特克利夫先生就会开口求婚。他想不到您会从中作梗，只要您不去招惹他、安分守己，他就绝不会朝您这头想。他求婚的时候您自然会知道；至少您姐姐若应了他，会告诉您。她若一口回绝，您就只消让她稳住，别的什么都不用做。您若见她迟疑，就必须不惜一切打断进去，用您的全部影响拦住她。所以要放胆去做，尽力而为。倘若一切都落了空，她仍旧黏着他，我就得打出最后一张牌——不如说，得由您替我打。我将留给您一封封好的信，万般皆败时，您就交给她。要赶在她第二次见拉特克利夫之前办。务必让她读；必要时，不管什么时候、什么地方，逼也要逼她读。绝不能让任何人知道有这么一封信，您要把它当一颗钻石那样经心。信里写了什么，您也不许知道；它必须是个彻底的秘密。您明白了吗？”

===Original===
Sybil thought she did, but her heart sank. “When shall you give me this letter?” she asked.
“The evening before I start, when I come to bid goodbye; probably next Sunday. This letter is our last hope. If, after reading that, she does not give him up, you will have to pack your trunk, my dear Sybil, and find a new home, for you can never live with them.”

===Chinese===
西比尔觉得自己明白了，心却往下一沉。“这封信，您几时给我？”她问。
“我动身的前一天晚上，我来辞行的时候；多半是下个礼拜天。这封信是我们最后的指望了。她读了信若还是不肯撒手，我亲爱的西比尔，您就只好收拾箱子，另找一个家了，因为您永远没法同他们一起过活。”

===Original===
He had never before called her by her first name, and it pleased her to hear it now, though she generally had a strong objection to such familiarities. “Oh, I wish you were not going!” she exclaimed tearfully. “What shall I do when you are gone?”

===Chinese===
他从没叫过她的教名，此刻听着很受用，尽管平日她对这类亲昵的称呼一向强烈反对。“哦，我多希望您不走！”她含着泪喊道。“您走了，我可怎么办呢？”

===Original===
At this pitiful appeal, Carrington felt a sudden pang. He found that he was not so old as he had thought. Certainly he had grown to like her frank honesty and sound common sense, and he had at length discovered that she was handsome, with a very pretty figure. Was it not something like a flirtation he had been carrying on with this young person for the last month? A glimmering of suspicion crossed his mind, though he got rid of it as quickly as possible. For a man of his age and sobriety to be in love with two sisters at once was impossible; still more impossible that Sybil should care for him.

===Chinese===
听了这可怜的一问，卡林顿心头猛地一痛。他发现自己并不像自以为的那么老。的确，他已渐渐喜欢上她的坦白诚实和健全的常识，而且终于发现她长得标致，身段也很美。这一个月来他同这位年轻小姐的往来，岂不有点像一场调情？一线疑影掠过他的心头，虽然他尽快把它摆脱了。像他这样年纪、这样稳重的人，同时爱上姐妹两个，是不可能的；西比尔会看上他，就更不可能了。

===Original===
As for her, however, there was no doubt about the matter. She had grown to depend upon him, and she did it with all the blind confidence of youth. To lose him was a serious disaster. She had never before felt the sensation, and she thought it most disagreeable. Her youthful diplomatists and admirers could not at all fill Carrington’s place. They danced and chirruped cheerfully on the hollow crust of society, but they were wholly useless when one suddenly fell through and found oneself struggling in the darkness and dangers beneath. Young women, too, are apt to be flattered by the confidences of older men; they have a keen palate for whatever savours of experience and adventure. For the first time in her life, Sybil had found a man who gave some play to her imagination; one who had been a rebel, and had grown used to the shocks of fate, so as to walk with calmness into the face of death, and to command or obey with equal indifference. She felt that he would tell her what to do when the earthquake came, and would be at hand to consult, which is in a woman’s eyes the great object of men’s existence, when trouble comes. She suddenly conceived that Washington would be intolerable without him, and that she should never get the courage to fight Mr. Ratcliffe alone, or, if she did, she should make some fatal mistake. They finished their ride very soberly. She began to show a new interest in all that concerned him, and asked many questions about his sisters and their plantation. She wanted to ask him whether she could not do something to help them, but this seemed too awkward. On his part he made her promise to write him faithfully all that took place, and this request pleased her, though she knew his interest was all on her sister’s account.

===Chinese===
至于她，这件事却没有疑问。她已渐渐依赖上他了，而且依得像青春年少那样，一概是盲目的信赖。失去他是一桩重大的灾难。这种滋味她从没尝过，只觉得说不出的难受。她那些年轻的外交官和崇拜者们，全然顶不了卡林顿的位置。他们在社会那层空心的薄壳上欢快地又跳又啭，可一旦人突然陷落下去、在底下的黑暗与危险里挣扎，他们就全然无用了。年轻的女人，又往往爱听年长的男人倾吐心事；凡是带着阅历和奇遇气味的东西，她们的口味都刁得很。平生头一回，西比尔遇见了一个能让她的想象力活动起来的男人；一个做过叛军的男人，受惯了命运的种种打击，能神色安详地迎着死亡走去，发号施令和俯首听命同样无所谓。她觉得，大地震来的时候，他会告诉她该怎么办，会守在近旁随时可以商量——在女人眼里，祸事临头时，这正是男人存在的头等大用。她忽然想明白了：没有他，华盛顿将叫人无法忍受；她永远鼓不起独自同拉特克利夫先生周旋的勇气，就算鼓起来，也会闯出致命的大错。他们非常肃穆地骑完了这一程。她开始对他的一切显出一种新的关切，问了他许多关于他妹妹们和她们庄园的事。她想问问他，自己能不能帮上什么忙，可这话似乎太着痕迹。他这边呢，则要她答应，往后发生的一切，都原原本本写信告诉他；这个请求使她高兴，虽然她知道，他的关心，全是为着她姐姐。

===Original===
The following Sunday evening when he came to bid goodbye, it was still worse. There was no chance for private talk. Ratcliffe was there, and several diplomatists, including old Jacobi, who had eyes like a cat and saw every motion of one’s face. Victoria Dare was on the sofa, chattering with Lord Dunbeg; Sybil would rather have had any ordinary illness, even to the extent of a light case of scarlet fever or smallpox than let her know what was the matter. Carrington found means to get Sybil into another room for a moment and to give her the letter he had promised. Then he bade her goodbye, and in doing so he reminded her of her promise to write, pressing her hand and looking into her eyes with an earnestness that made her heart beat faster, although she said to herself that his interest was all about her sister; as it was﻿—mostly. The thought did not raise her spirits, but she went through with her performance like a heroine. Perhaps she was a little pleased to see that he parted from Madeleine with much less apparent feeling. One would have said that they were two good friends who had no troublesome sentiment to worry them. But then every eye in the room was watching this farewell, and speculating about it. Ratcliffe looked on with particular interest and was a little perplexed to account for this too fraternal cordiality. Could he have made a miscalculation? or was there something behind? He himself insisted upon shaking hands genially with Carrington and wished him a pleasant journey and a successful one.

===Chinese===
到了下个礼拜天晚上他来辞行的时候，情形更糟了。私下谈话的机会一点也没有。拉特克利夫在场，还有几位外交官，包括老雅各比——他长着一双猫似的眼睛，人脸上的一颦一动都逃不过。维多利亚·戴尔坐在沙发上，正同邓贝格勋爵喋喋不休；西比尔宁可害一场寻常的病，哪怕害一场轻症的猩红热或天花，也不愿让她知道底细。卡林顿寻了个法子，把西比尔带到另一间屋里待了片刻，把答应的信交给了她。随后他向她道别，道别时又提醒她写信的诺言，握着她的手，注视着她的眼睛，那份恳切叫她的心跳得快了起来，虽然她对自己说，他的关切全在她姐姐身上；事实也确是如此——大体如此。这个念头并未使她振奋，但她像个女英雄一般，把自己的戏演完了。看到他同马德琳分手时外表的感情淡得多，她或许还有几分得意。人家准会当他们是两个好朋友，没有什么恼人的情意来烦扰。可屋里每一双眼睛都盯着这场道别，对它猜三想四。拉特克利夫看得格外留心，对这份近乎手足之情的亲热，一时有点解释不过来。莫非他失算了？还是这背后另有什么文章？他自己执意同卡林顿亲亲热热地握了手，祝他旅途愉快，一路功成。

===Original===
That night, for the first time since she was a child, Sybil actually cried a little after she went to bed, although it is true that her sentiment did not keep her awake. She felt lonely and weighed down by a great responsibility. For a day or two afterwards she was nervous and restless. She would not ride, or make calls, or see guests. She tried to sing a little, and found it tiresome. She went out and sat for hours in the Square, where the spring sun was shining warm and bright on the prancing horse of the great Andrew Jackson. She was a little cross, too, and absent, and spoke so often about Carrington that at last Madeleine was struck by sudden suspicion, and began to watch her with anxious care.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，西比尔上床之后当真哭了一小会儿——打小时候以来这还是头一遭，虽说这份伤感确实没叫她睡不着。她觉得孤单，又压着一副天大的责任。此后有一两天，她烦躁不安，坐立不定。她不肯骑马，不肯拜客，也不肯见客。她试着唱了唱，觉得腻味。她出门在广场上一坐几个钟头，春日的太阳正暖融融、亮堂堂地照着伟大的安德鲁·杰克逊那匹昂首腾跃的骏马。她还脾气有点坏，神思恍惚，卡林顿长卡林顿短地说个不停，说得马德琳终于蓦地起了疑心，开始提心吊胆地留意起她来。

===Original===
Tuesday night, after this had gone on for two days, Sybil was in Madeleine’s room, where she often stayed to talk while her sister was at her toilet. This evening she threw herself listlessly on the couch, and within five minutes again quoted Carrington. Madeleine turned from the glass before which she was sitting, and looked her steadily in the face.
“Sybil,” said she, “this is the twenty-fourth time you have mentioned Mr. Carrington since we sat down to dinner. I have waited for the round number to decide whether I should take any notice of it or not? what does it mean, my child? Do you care for Mr. Carrington?”

===Chinese===
这样过了两天，礼拜二晚上，西比尔在马德琳房里——姐姐梳妆的时候，她常待在那儿陪她说话。这天晚上，她没精打采地往躺椅上一倒，不出五分钟，又引用起卡林顿来。马德琳从面前的穿衣镜前转过身，直直地望着她的脸。
“西比尔，”她说，“从坐下吃晚饭到这会儿，你已经第二十四次提起卡林顿先生了。我特意等到这个整数，才好决定要不要过问。这是什么意思，我的孩子？你心里是不是有卡林顿先生？”

===Original===
“Oh, Maude!” exclaimed Sybil reproachfully, flushing so violently that, even by that dim light, her sister could not but see it.
Mrs. Lee rose and, crossing the room, sat down by Sybil who was lying on the couch and turned her face away. Madeleine put her arms round her neck and kissed her.

===Chinese===
“哦，莫德！”西比尔嗔怪地喊出声来，脸红得那样厉害，即便在那样昏暗的光线里，她姐姐也不能不看见。
李夫人站起身，穿过房间，挨着西比尔坐下——她躺在躺椅上，把脸扭向了一边。马德琳伸出双臂搂住她的脖子，吻了她。

===Original===
“My poor﻿—poor child!” said she pityingly. “I never dreamed of this! What a fool I have been! How could I have been so thoughtless! Tell me!” she added, with a little hesitation; “has he﻿—does he care for you?”

===Chinese===
“我可怜的——可怜的孩子！”她怜惜地说。“我做梦也没想到这一层！我多傻呀！我怎么会这样粗心！告诉我！”她略略迟疑，又添了一句；“他——他心里有你吗？”

===Original===
“No! no!” cried Sybil, fairly breaking down into a burst of tears; “no! he loves you! nobody but you! he never gave a thought to me. I don’t care for him so very much,” she continued, drying her tears; “only it seems so lonely now he is gone.”

===Chinese===
“不！不！”西比尔喊道，索性放声哭了出来；“不！他爱的是你！除了你没有别人！他从来没有把我放在心上。我并不怎么在乎他，”她拭着眼泪接着说；“只是他走了，如今显得这样冷清。”

===Original===
Mrs. Lee remained on the couch, with her arm round her sister’s neck, silent, gazing into vacancy, the picture of perplexity and consternation. The situation was getting beyond her control.

===Chinese===
李夫人仍旧坐在躺椅上，一条胳膊搂着妹妹的脖子，默然无语，凝望着虚空，整个人是一幅惶惑惊愕的写照。局势正在渐渐脱出她的掌握。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
In the middle of April a sudden social excitement started the indolent city of Washington to its feet. The Grand-Duke and Duchess of Saxe-Baden-Hombourg arrived in America on a tour of pleasure, and in due course came on to pay their respects to the Chief Magistrate of the Union.
